 138: 
 139:    We reserve the letters p and q for prime numbers. We employ the Landau­Bachmann
 140: "Big Oh" notation O, as well as the associated Vinogradov symbols  and , with their
 141: usual meanings. Any dependence of the implied constants is explicitly stated or indicated
 142: with subscripts. In particular, notations like Ou and u are shortcuts for Oa1,a2 and a1,a2,
 143: respectively. We let i denote the imaginary unity, and we put n := e2i/n for every positive
 144: integer n. For every field F , we let F n := {an : a  F }. If F is a number field, we write F
 145: for the discriminant of F over Q, we let NF (a) be the norm of a  K over Q, and we write
 146: a1/n for an arbitrary, but fixed, nth root of a (in some extension of F ). Moreover, (n), µ(n),
 147: and p(n) denote the Euler totient function, the M¨obius function, and the p-adic valuation, of
 148: a positive integer n. Given a Galois extension E/F of number fields, a prime ideal p of OF
 149: that does not ramify in E, and a prime ideal P of OE lying over p, we write E/F | P for the
 150: Frobenius automorphism corresponding to P/p, that is, the unique   Gal(E/F ) such that
 151: (a)  aNF (p) (mod P) for every a  OE, where NF (p) denotes the norm of p in F . Moreover,
 152: we let E/F | p be the set of all E/F | P , where P runs over the prime ideals of OE lying
 153: over p.

 157: 3.1. Preliminaries. We need the following conditional version of the Chebotarev density
 158: theorem.

 160: Theorem 3.1. Assume the GRH. Let F/Q be a finite Galois extension with Galois group G,
 161: and let C be a union of conjugacy classes of G. Then we have that
 162: 
 163:                 F,C(x) := # p  x : p does not ramify in F and F/Q | p  C
 164: 
 165:                 =  #C      x  x     +  O  #C x1/2    log |F |  +  log  x  ,
 166:                    #G    log                           #G
 167: 
 168: for all x > 1.
 169: 
 170: Proof. See, e.g., [10, Chapter 2, Section 7].
 171: 
 172: We also need some results on the degree and the discriminant of certain number fields.
 173: 
 174: Lemma 3.2. Let F be a number field, let a  F  with a not a root of unity, and let n be a
 175: positive integer. Then:
 176: 
 177:      (i) [F (n, a1/n) : Q] F,a (n)n;
 178: 
 179:     (ii) log |F (n,a1/n)| F,a (n)n log(2n);
 180:    (iii) Each prime factor of F (n,a1/n) divides NF (a)F n.
 181: 
 182: Proof. See, e.g., [17, Lemma 3 and Lemma 5]. (Claim (iii) is implicit in the proof of [17,
 183: Lemma 5].)

 198: square in Z, so that K := Q( Du) is a quadratic number field. Let ,   K be the two roots
 199: of fu, and put  := /. Note that NK () = 1. Finally, assume that u is nondegenerate, that
 200: is,  is not a root of unity.
 201:

 203: Indeed, writing  = a + b K with a, b  Q, we have that Kn is the splitting field of
 204: X2n - 2aXn + 1. Furthermore, let Cn  Gal(Kn/Q) be defined as Cn := {id, }, if there exists
 205:   Gal(Kn/Q) such that (n) = n-1 and (1/n) = -1/n, and as Cn := {id}, if such 
 206: does not exist. Note that Cn is a union of conjugacy classes, since  belongs to the center of
 207: Gal(Kn/Q).

function Read-GzipText([string]$Path) {
 $fs=[IO.File]::OpenRead($Path)
 try {
  $gz=New-Object IO.Compression.GzipStream($fs,[IO.Compression.CompressionMode]::Decompress)
  try { $sr=New-Object IO.StreamReader($gz,[Text.Encoding]::UTF8); try { return $sr.ReadToEnd() } finally {$sr.Dispose()} } finally {$gz.Dispose()}
 } finally {$fs.Dispose()}
}

   47: \begin{abstract}
   48: Let $U = (U_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a Lucas sequence and, for every prime number $p$, let $\rho_U(p)$ be the rank of appearance of $p$ in $U$, that is, the smallest positive integer $k$ such that $p$ divides $U_k$, whenever it exists.
   49: Furthermore, let $d$ be an odd positive integer.
   50: Under some mild hypotheses, we prove an asymptotic formula for the number of primes $p \leq x$ such that $d$ divides $\rho_U(p)$, as $x \to +\infty$.
   51: \end{abstract}
   52: 
   53: \section{Introduction}
   54: 
   55: Let $(U_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a Lucas sequence, that is, a sequence of integers satisfying $U_0 = 0$, $U_1 = 1$, and $U_n = a_1 U_{n - 1} + a_2 U_{n - 2}$ for every integer $n \geq 2$, where $a_1, a_2$ are fixed nonzero integers.
   56: The \emph{rank of appearance} of a prime number $p$, denoted by $\rho_U(p)$, is the smallest positive integer $k$ such that $p \mid U_k$.
   57: It can be easily seen that $\rho_U(p)$ exists whenever $p \nmid a_2$.
-----
   67: 
   68: Let $\alpha, \beta$ be the roots of the characteristic polynomial $f_U(X) := X^2 - a_1 X - a_2$, and assume that $\gamma := \alpha / \beta$ is not a root of unity.
   69: Let $\Delta := a_1^2 + 4a_2$ be the discriminant of $f_U(X)$, and let $\Delta_0$ be the squarefree part of $\Delta$.
   70: Assume that $\Delta$ is not a square, so that $K := \mathbb{Q}\big(\!\sqrt{\Delta}\big)$ is a quadratic number field.
   71: Let $h$ be the greatest positive integer such that $\gamma$ is a $h$th power in $K$.
   72: 
   73: Our result is the following:
   74: 
   75: \begin{thm}\label{thm:main}
   76: Let $d$ be an odd positive integer with $3 \nmid d$ whenever $\Delta_0 = -3$.
   77: Then, for every $x > \exp\!\big(Be^{8\omega(d)} d^8 \big)$, we have
   78: \begin{equation*}
   79: \mathcal{R}_U(d; x) = \delta_{U}(d) \Li(x) + O_U\!\left(\frac{(\omega(d) + 1)d}{\varphi(d)} \cdot \frac{x\,(\log \log x)^{\omega(d)}}{(\log x)^{9/8}}\right) ,
   80: \end{equation*}
   81: where $B > 0$ is an absolute constant and
   82: \begin{equation*}
   83: \delta_U(d) := \frac1{d}\left(\frac1{(d^\infty, h)} + \eta_U(d)\right) \prod_{p \,\mid\, d} \left(1 - \frac1{p^2}\right)^{-1} ,
   84: \end{equation*}
   85: with $\eta_U(d) := 0$ if $\Delta > 0$ or $\Delta_0 \not\equiv 1 \pmod 4$ or $\Delta_0 \nmid d^\infty$; and
   86: \begin{equation*}
   87: \eta_U(d) := \frac{(d^\infty, h)}{\big[(d^\infty, h), \Delta_0 / (d, \Delta_0)\big]^2}
   88: \end{equation*}
   89: otherwise.
   90: \end{thm}
   91: 
   92: Cubre and Rouse's proof of the asymptotic formula for $\mathcal{R}_F(d; x)$ relies on the study of the algebraic group $G : x^2 - 5y^2 = 1$ and relates $\rho_F(p)$ with the order of $(3/2,1/2) \in G(\mathbb{F}_p)$.
   93: Instead, our proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main} is an adaptation of the methods that Moree~\cite{MR2274151} used to prove an asymptotic formula for the number of primes $p \leq x$ such that the multiplicative order of $g$ modulo $p$ is divisible by $d$, where $g \notin \{-1, 0, +1\}$ is a fixed rational number.

  103: In particular, notations like $O_U$ and $\ll_U$ are shortcuts for $O_{a_1,a_2}$ and $\ll_{a_1,a_2}$, respectively.

   86: \begin{equation*}
   87: \eta_U(d) := \frac{(d^\infty, h)}{\big[(d^\infty, h), \Delta_0 / (d, \Delta_0)\big]^2}
   88: \end{equation*}
   89: otherwise.
   90: \end{thm}
   91: 
   92: Cubre and Rouse's proof of the asymptotic formula for $\mathcal{R}_F(d; x)$ relies on the study of the algebraic group $G : x^2 - 5y^2 = 1$ and relates $\rho_F(p)$ with the order of $(3/2,1/2) \in G(\mathbb{F}_p)$.
   93: Instead, our proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main} is an adaptation of the methods that Moree~\cite{MR2274151} used to prove an asymptotic formula for the number of primes $p \leq x$ such that the multiplicative order of $g$ modulo $p$ is divisible by $d$, where $g \notin \{-1, 0, +1\}$ is a fixed rational number.

   97: The author thanks Laura~Capuano (Politecnico di Torino) for several helpful discussions concerning Lemma~\ref{lem:Knd}.
   98: 
   99: \section{Notation}
  100: 
  101: We employ the Landau--Bachmann ``Big Oh'' notation $O$, as well as the associated Vinogradov symbol $\ll$. 
  102: Any dependence of the implied constants is explicitly stated or indicated with subscripts. 
  103: In particular, notations like $O_U$ and $\ll_U$ are shortcuts for $O_{a_1,a_2}$ and $\ll_{a_1,a_2}$, respectively.
  104: For $x \geq 2$ we let $\Li(x) := \int_2^x \!\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\log t}$ denote the logarithmic integral.
  105: We reserve the letter $p$ for prime numbers.
  106: Given an integer $d$, we let $d^\infty$ denote the supernatural number $\prod_{p \mid d} p^\infty$.
  107: Given a field $F$ and a positive integer $n$, we write $F^n$ for the set of $n$th powers of elements of $F$.
  108: Given a Galois extension $E/F$ of number fields and a prime ideal $P$ of $\mathcal{O}_E$ lying above an unramified prime ideal $\mathfrak{p}$ of $\mathcal{O}_F$, we write $\Big[\frac{E / F}{P}\Big]$ for the Frobenius automorphism corresponding to $P / \mathfrak{p}$, that is, the unique element $\sigma$ of the Galois group $\Gal(E / F)$ that satisfies $\sigma(a) \equiv a^{N(\mathfrak{p})} \pmod P$ for every $a \in \mathcal{O}_E$, where $N(\mathfrak{p})$ denotes the norm of $\mathfrak{p}$.
  109: Moreover, we let $\Big[\frac{E / F}{\mathfrak{p}}\Big]$ be the set of all $\Big[\frac{E / F}{P}\Big]$ with $P$ prime ideal of $\mathcal{O}_E$ lying over $\mathfrak{p}$.
  110: We write $\Delta_{E/F}$ for the relative discriminant of $E/F$, and $\Delta_E := \Delta_{E/\mathbb{Q}}$ for the absolute discriminant of $E$.
  111: For every integer $d$ and for every prime number $p$ we let $\big(\tfrac{d}{p}\big)$ be the Legendre symbol.
  112: For every positive integer $n$, we let $\zeta_n := \mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathbf{i} / n}$ be a primitive $n$th root of unity.
  113: We write $\omega(n)$, $\varphi(n)$, $\mu(n)$, and $\tau(n)$, for the number of prime factors, the totient function, the M\"obius function, and the number of divisors of a positive integer $n$, respectively.
  114: 
  115: \section{General preliminaries}

  117: \begin{lem}\label{lem:zetanmodP}
  118: Let $n$ be a positive integer, let $p$ be a prime number not dividing $n$, and let $P$ be a prime ideal of $\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_n)}$ lying over $p$.
  119: Then $\zeta_n$ has multiplicative order modulo $P$ equal to $n$.
  120: \end{lem}

  166: where $n_E := [E : \mathbb{Q}]$ and $c_1, c_2 > 0$ are absolute constants.

  172: \section{Preliminaries to the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main}}
  173: 
  174: Recalling that $h$ is the greatest positive integer such that $\gamma$ is an $h$th power in $K$, write $\gamma = \gamma_0^h$ for some $\gamma_0 \in K$.
  175: Also, let $\sigma_K \in \Gal(K / \mathbb{Q})$ be the nontrivial automorphism, which satisfies $\sigma_K\big(\!\sqrt{\Delta}\;\!\big) = -\sqrt{\Delta}$.
  176: Note that, since $\gamma = \alpha/\beta$ and $\sigma_K$ swaps $\alpha$ and $\beta$, we have that $\sigma_k(\gamma) = \gamma^{-1}$.
  177: For all positive integers $d, n$ such that $d \mid n$, let $K_{n, d} := K\big(\zeta_n, \gamma^{1/d}\big)$.
  178: 
  179: \begin{lem}\label{lem:order}
  180: Let $p$ be a prime number not dividing $a_2 \Delta$ and let $\pi$ be a prime ideal of $\mathcal{O}_K$ lying over $p$.
  181: Then $\rho_U(p)$ is equal to the multiplicative order of $\gamma$ modulo $\pi$.
  182: Moreover, $\rho_U(p)$ divides $p - \big(\tfrac{\Delta}{p}\big)$.
  183: \end{lem}
  184: \begin{proof}
  185: First, note that $p \nmid a_2$ ensures that $\beta$ is invertible modulo $\pi$, and consequently it makes sense to consider the multiplicative order of $\gamma = \alpha / \beta$ modulo $\pi$.
  186: Also, $p \nmid \Delta$ implies that $p$ does not ramifies in $K$ and that $\alpha \not\equiv \beta \pmod \pi$.
  187: 
  188: We shall prove that $p \mid U_n$ if and only if $\gamma^n \equiv 1 \pmod \pi$, for every positive integer $n$.
  189: Then the claim on $\rho_U(p)$ follows easily.
  190: It is well known that the Binet's formula
  191: \begin{equation}\label{equ:binet}
  192: U_n = \frac{\alpha^n - \beta^n}{\alpha - \beta}
  193: \end{equation}
  194: holds for every positive integer $n$.
  195: On the one hand, if $p \mid U_n$ then, since $p \mathcal{O}_K \subseteq \pi$ and~\eqref{equ:binet}, we have $\alpha^n \equiv \beta^n \pmod \pi$, and consequently $\gamma^n \equiv 1 \pmod \pi$.
  196: On the other hand, if $\gamma^n \equiv 1 \pmod \pi$ then by~\eqref{equ:binet} we get $U_n \equiv 0 \pmod \pi$.
  197: If $p$ is inert in $K$, then $p\mathcal{O}_K = \pi$ and so $p \mid U_n$.
  198: If $p$ splits in $K$, then $p\mathcal{O}_K = \pi \cap \sigma_K(\pi)$.
  199: Thus $U_n \equiv 0 \pmod \pi$ and $U_n \equiv \sigma_K(U_k) \equiv 0 \pmod{\sigma_K(\pi)}$ imply that $p \mid U_n$.
  200: 
-----
  369: (\sigma_1 f)(X) = X^{d_0} - \sigma_1(\gamma_0^{h_0}) = X^{d_0} - N_{K / \mathbb{Q}}(\gamma_0^{h_0}) \gamma_0^{-h_0} = X^{d_0} - \gamma_0^{-h_0} ,
  370: \end{equation*}
  371: and so $\sigma$ satisfies~\eqref{equ:sigma}.
  372: Pick $\sigma_0 \in \Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_n)/\mathbb{Q})$ such that $\sigma_0(\zeta_n) = \zeta_n^{-1}$.
  373: If $\sqrt{\Delta} \in \mathbb{Q}(\zeta_n)$ then $K(\zeta_n) = \mathbb{Q}(\zeta_n)$, $\Delta < 0$, and $\sigma_0\big(\!\sqrt{\Delta}\big) = \overline{\sqrt{\Delta}} = -\sqrt{\Delta}$, so we let $\sigma_1 := \sigma_0$.
  374: If $\sqrt{\Delta} \notin \mathbb{Q}(\zeta_n)$ then $X^2 - \Delta$ is the minimal polynomial of $\sqrt{\Delta}$ over $\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_n)$ and we can extend $\sigma_0$ to $\sigma_1 \in \Gal(K(\zeta_n) / \mathbb{Q})$ such that $\sigma_1\big(\!\sqrt{\Delta}\big) = -\sqrt{\Delta}$.
  375: \end{proof}

  377: \section{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main}}
  378: 
  379: The proof proceeds similarly to~\cite[Section~2]{MR2274151}.
  380: For all positive integers $d, n$ with $d \mid n$, and for all $x > 1$, let us define
  381: \begin{equation*}
  382: \pi_{U, n, d}(x) := \#\big\{p \leq x : p \nmid a_2\Delta,\, p \equiv \big(\tfrac{\Delta}{p}\big) \!\!\! \pmod n, \, d \mid \iota_U(p)\big\} .
  383: \end{equation*}
  384: In what follows, we will tacitly ignore the finitely many prime numbers dividing $a_2 \Delta$.
  385: 
  386: \begin{lem}\label{lem:RUdx}
  387: For every positive integer $d$ and for every $x > 1$, we have
  388: \begin{equation}\label{equ:Rdoublesum}
  389: \mathcal{R}_U(d; x) = \!\!\!\sum_{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty} \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \pi_{U, dv, av}(x) .
  390: \end{equation}
  391: \end{lem}
  392: \begin{proof}
  393: Every prime number $p$ counted by the inner sum of~\eqref{equ:Rdoublesum} satisfies $p \leq x$, $p \equiv \big(\tfrac{\Delta}{p}\big) \pmod{dv}$, and $\iota_U(p) = v w$ for some integer $w$.
  394: Writing $w = w_1 w_2$, with $w_1 := (w, d)$, we get that the contribution of $p$ to the inner sum or~\eqref{equ:Rdoublesum} is equal to $\sum_{a \mid w_1} \mu(a)$.
  395: Hence,
  396: \begin{equation}\label{equ:Rsinglesum}
  397: \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \pi_{U, dv, av}(x) = \#\big\{p \leq x : p \equiv \big(\tfrac{\Delta}{p}\big) \!\!\!\pmod{dv}, \, v \mid \iota_U(p), \, \big(\iota_U(p) / v, d\big) = 1\big\} .
  398: \end{equation}
  399: Now it suffices to show that
  400: \begin{equation}\label{equ:Rdoublesum1}
  401: \mathcal{R}_U(d; x) = \!\!\!\sum_{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty} \#\big\{p \leq x : p \equiv \big(\tfrac{\Delta}{p}\big) \!\!\!\pmod{dv}, \, v \mid \iota_U(p), \, \big(\iota_U(p) / v, d\big) = 1\big\} .
  402: \end{equation}
  403: On the one hand, let $p$ be a prime number counted on the right-hand side of~\eqref{equ:Rdoublesum1}.
  404: Note that this is counted only one, namely for $v = (\iota_U(p), d^\infty)$.
  405: Then, from $\rho_U(p) \iota_U(p) = p - \big(\tfrac{\Delta}{p}\big)$, it follows that $d \mid \rho_U(p)$.
-----
  412: \end{proof}

  414: \begin{lem}\label{lem:piUnd}
  415: Let $n$ be an odd positive integer with $3 \nmid n$ whenever $\Delta_0 = -3$, and let $d$ be a positive integer dividing $n$.
  416: There exist absolute constants $A, B > 0$ such that
  417: \begin{equation*}
  418: \pi_{U, n, d}(x) = \delta_{U,n,d} \Li(x) + O_U\!\left(x \exp\big({-A}(\log x)^{1/2} / n \big)\right)
  419: \end{equation*}
  420: for $x \geq \exp(B n^8)$, where
  421: \begin{equation}\label{equ:defdeltaUnd}
  422: \delta_{U,n,d} := \frac{(d, h)}{\varphi(n)d} \cdot \begin{cases} 1 &\text{ if $\Delta > 0$ or $\Delta_0 \not\equiv 1 \!\!\!\pmod 4$ or $\Delta_0 \nmid n$},\\ 2 &\text{ otherwise}. \end{cases}
  423: \end{equation}
  424: \end{lem}
  425: \begin{proof}
  426: Put $E := K_{n,d}$, $F := \mathbb{Q}$, $G := \Gal(E/F)$, and $C = \{\id, \sigma\}$ if there exists $\sigma \in \Gal(K_{n,d} / \mathbb{Q})$ satisfying~\eqref{equ:sigma}, or $C = \{\id\}$ otherwise.
  427: By Lemma~\ref{lem:Knd}, $\sigma$ belongs to the center of $G$, so that $C$ is the union of conjugacy classes of $G$.
  428: By Lemma~\ref{lem:iota}, we have that $\pi_{U,n,d}(x)$ is the number of primes $p$ not exceeding $x$ and such that $\Big[\frac{E/F}{p}\Big] \subseteq C$.
  429: Thus, taking into account the bounds for the degree and the discriminant of $E / F$ given in Lemma~\ref{lem:Knd}, and considering Lemma~\ref{lem:sqrt}, the asymptotic formula follows by applying Theorem~\ref{thm:chebotarev}.
  430: \end{proof}
  431: 
  432: \begin{lem}\label{lem:bounds}
  433: Let $d$ be an odd positive integer with $3 \nmid d$ whenever $\Delta_0 = -3$.
  434: If $x > 1$ and $e^{\omega(d)} \leq y \leq \log x / \varphi(d)$, then
  435: \begin{equation}\label{equ:firstbound}
  436: \sum_{\substack{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty \\[1pt] v \,>\, y}} \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \pi_{U, dv, av}(x) \ll \frac{x}{\log x} \cdot \frac{\omega(d) + 1}{\varphi(d)} \cdot \frac{(\log y)^{\omega(d)}}{y}
  437: \end{equation}
  438: and
  439: \begin{equation*}
  440: \sum_{\substack{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty \\[1pt] v \,>\, y}} \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \delta_{U, dv, av} \ll_U \frac{\omega(d) + 1}{\varphi(d)} \cdot \frac{(\log y)^{\omega(d)}}{y} .
  441: \end{equation*}
  442: \end{lem}
  443: \begin{proof}
  444: Let $\pi(m, r; x) := \#\{p \leq x : p \equiv r \pmod m\}$.
  445: From~\eqref{equ:Rsinglesum} it follows that
  446: \begin{equation}\label{equ:sumadmuboundpi}
  447: \left|\sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \pi_{U, dv, av}(x)\right| \leq \pi_{U, dv, v}(x) \leq \pi(x; dv, \pm 1) .
  448: \end{equation}
-----
  498: \begin{cases}S_{d, 1, h}  & \text{ if $\Delta > 0$ or $\Delta_0 \not\equiv 1 \!\!\!\pmod 4$ or $\Delta_0 \nmid d^\infty$}\\
  499: S_{d, 1, h} + S_{d, e, h} & \text{ otherwise}
  500: \end{cases}
  501: = \delta_U(d) ,
  502: \end{equation*}
  503: as claimed.
  504: \end{proof}
  505: 
  506: \begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main}]
  507: Let $A, B > 0$ be the constants of Lemma~\ref{lem:piUnd}.
  508: Assume that $x \geq \exp\big(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8\big)$ and put $y := (\log x / B)^{1/8} / d$.
  509: Note that $e^{\omega(d)} \leq y \leq \log x / \varphi(d)$ and $\log y \leq \log \log x$, for every $x \gg_B 1$.
  510: By~Lemma~\ref{lem:RUdx}, Lemma~\ref{lem:piUnd}, and Lemma~\ref{lem:series}, we obtain that
  511: \begin{align*}
  512: \mathcal{R}_U(d; x) &= \sum_{\substack{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty \\[1pt] v \,\leq\, y}} \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \pi_{U, dv, av}(x) + O(E_1) \\
  513: &= \sum_{\substack{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty \\[1pt] v \,\leq\, y}} \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \delta_{U, dv, av} \Li(x) + O(E_1) + O_U(E_2) \\
  514: &= \delta_U(d) \Li(x) + O(E_1) + O_U(E_2) + O(E_3) ,
  515: \end{align*}
  516: where, by Lemma~\ref{lem:bounds}, we have
  517: \begin{equation*}
  518: E_1 := \sum_{\substack{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty \\[1pt] v \,>\, y}} \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \pi_{U, dv, av}(x) \ll \frac{x}{\log x} \cdot \frac{\omega(d) + 1}{\varphi(d)} \cdot \frac{(\log y)^{\omega(d)}}{y} \ll \frac{(\omega(d) + 1) d}{\varphi(d)} \cdot \frac{x\,(\log \log x)^{\omega(d)}}{(\log x)^{9/8}}
  519: \end{equation*}
  520: and
  521: \begin{equation*}
  522: E_3 := \sum_{\substack{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty \\[1pt] v \,>\, y}} \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} \mu(a) \delta_{U, dv, av} \Li(x) \ll_U \frac{\omega(d) + 1}{\varphi(d)} \cdot \frac{(\log y)^{\omega(d)}}{y} \cdot \Li(x) \ll \frac{(\omega(d) + 1) d}{\varphi(d)} \cdot \frac{x\,(\log \log x)^{\omega(d)}}{(\log x)^{9/8}} ,
  523: \end{equation*}
  524: while, also using the inequality $\tau(d) / d \leq d / \varphi(d)$, we have
  525: \begin{align*}
  526: E_2 &:= \sum_{\substack{\;\;\;v \,\mid\, d^\infty \\[1pt] v \,\leq\, y}} \sum_{a \,\mid\, d} x \exp\big({-A}(\log x)^{1/2} / (dv) \big) \ll x \exp\big({-A}B^{1/8}(\log x)^{3/8} \big) \tau(d) y \\
  527: &\ll x \exp\big({-A}B^{1/8}(\log x)^{3/8} \big) (\log x)^{1/8} \cdot \frac{\tau(d)}{d} \ll \frac{d}{\varphi(d)} \cdot \frac{x}{(\log x)^{9/8}} .
  528: \end{align*}
  529: The result follows.
  530: \end{proof}

   51: \begin{abstract}
   52:     Let $\bm{u} = (u_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a Lucas sequence, that is, a sequence of integers satisfying $u_0 = 0$, $u_1 = 1$, and $u_n = a_1 u_{n - 1} + a_2 u_{n - 2}$ for every integer $n \geq 2$, where $a_1$ and $a_2$ are fixed nonzero integers.
   53:     For each prime number $p$ with $p \nmid 2a_2D_{\bm{u}}$, where $D_{\bm{u}} := a_1^2 + 4a_2$, let $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ be the \emph{rank of appearance} of $p$ in $\bm{u}$, that is, the smallest positive integer $k$ such that $p \mid u_k$.
   54:     It is well known that $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ exists and that $p \equiv \big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p \big) \pmod {\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)}$, where $\big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p \big)$ is the Legendre symbol.
   55:     Define the \emph{index of appearance} of $p$ in $\bm{u}$ as $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p) := \left(p - \big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p \big)\right) / \rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$.
   56:     For each positive integer $t$ and for every $x > 0$, let $\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x)$ be the set of prime numbers $p$ such that $p \leq x$, $p \nmid 2a_2 D_{\bm{u}}$, and $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p) = t$.
   57: 
   58:     Under the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis, and under some mild assumptions on $\bm{u}$, we prove that
   59: 	\begin{equation*}
   60:         \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x) = A\, F_{\bm{u}}(t) \, G_{\bm{u}}(t) \, \frac{x}{\log x} + O_{\bm{u}}\!\left(\frac{x}{(\log x)^2} + \frac{x \log (2\log x)}{\varphi(t) (\log x)^2}\right) ,
   61:     \end{equation*}
   62:     for all positive integers $t$ and for all $x > t^3$, where $A$ is the Artin constant, $F_{\bm{u}}(\cdot)$ is a multiplicative function, and $G_{\bm{u}}(\cdot)$ is a periodic function (both these functions are effectively computable in terms of $\bm{u}$).
   63:     Furthermore, we provide some explicit examples and numerical data.

   68: \section{Introduction}
   69: 
   70: Let $\bm{u} = (u_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a Lucas sequence, that is, a sequence of integers satisfying $u_0 = 0$, $u_1 = 1$, and $u_n = a_1 u_{n - 1} + a_2 u_{n - 2}$ for every integer $n \geq 2$, where $a_1$ and $a_2$ are fixed nonzero integers.
   71: For each prime number $p$ with $p \nmid a_2$, let $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ be the \emph{rank of appearance} of $p$ in $\bm{u}$, that is, the smallest positive integer $k$ such that $p \mid u_k$.
   72: It is well known that $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ exists (for this and other elementary facts on $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ mentioned in the introduction, see~\cite[Chapter~1]{MR1761897}).
   73: Furthermore, we have that $p \equiv \big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p\big) \pmod {\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)}$ for every prime number $p$ with $p \nmid 2 a_2 D_{\bm{u}}$, where $D_{\bm{u}} := a_1^2 + 4a_2$ and $\big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p\big)$ is the Legendre symbol.
   74: We define the \emph{index of appearance} of $p$ in $\bm{u}$ as $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p) := \left(p - \big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p \big)\right) / \rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$, which, by the previous consideration, is a positive integer.
   75: Moreover, for each positive integer $t$ and for every $x > 0$, we define
   76: \begin{equation*}
   77: 	\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x) := \big\{p \leq x : p \nmid 2 a_2 D_{\bm{u}},\, \iota_{\bm{u}}(p) = t \big\} .
   78: \end{equation*}
   79: Note that $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$, respectively $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p)$, is somehow analog to the multiplicative order $r_g(p)$ modulo $p$, respectively the residual index $i_g(p) := (p - 1) / r_g(p)$ modulo $p$, of a rational number $g$ (assuming that $p$ does not divide the numerator and the denominator of $g$).
   80: In particular, the set $\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x)$ is analog to the set of prime numbers $p$ for which $g$ is a \emph{near-primitive root} modulo $p$, that is, $i_p(g) = t$ for a fixed $t$.
   81: This latter set has been studied by several authors \cite{MR3286520,MR1767657,MR2035537,MR3086966} (see also \cite{MR4231532,MR2314267} for generalizations to number fields).
   82: 
   83: As a first result, we provide a (conditional) asymptotic formula for $\#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x)$.
   84: We remark that the proof is a close adaptation of Hooley's proof of the Artin's conjecture under the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis (GRH)~\cite{MR207630}.
   85: 
   86: \begin{theorem}\label{thm:asymptotic}
   87: 	Assume the GRH.
   88: 	Let $\bm{u} = (u_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a nondegenerate Lucas sequence with nonsquare $D_{\bm{u}}$.
   89: 	Then, for every positive integer $t$, there exists $\delta_{\bm{u}}(t) \geq 0$ such that
   90: 	\begin{equation*}
-----
   76: \begin{equation*}
   77: 	\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x) := \big\{p \leq x : p \nmid 2 a_2 D_{\bm{u}},\, \iota_{\bm{u}}(p) = t \big\} .
   78: \end{equation*}
   79: Note that $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$, respectively $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p)$, is somehow analog to the multiplicative order $r_g(p)$ modulo $p$, respectively the residual index $i_g(p) := (p - 1) / r_g(p)$ modulo $p$, of a rational number $g$ (assuming that $p$ does not divide the numerator and the denominator of $g$).
   80: In particular, the set $\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x)$ is analog to the set of prime numbers $p$ for which $g$ is a \emph{near-primitive root} modulo $p$, that is, $i_p(g) = t$ for a fixed $t$.
   81: This latter set has been studied by several authors \cite{MR3286520,MR1767657,MR2035537,MR3086966} (see also \cite{MR4231532,MR2314267} for generalizations to number fields).
   82: 
   83: As a first result, we provide a (conditional) asymptotic formula for $\#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x)$.
   84: We remark that the proof is a close adaptation of Hooley's proof of the Artin's conjecture under the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis (GRH)~\cite{MR207630}.
   85: 
   86: \begin{theorem}\label{thm:asymptotic}
   87: 	Assume the GRH.
   88: 	Let $\bm{u} = (u_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a nondegenerate Lucas sequence with nonsquare $D_{\bm{u}}$.
   89: 	Then, for every positive integer $t$, there exists $\delta_{\bm{u}}(t) \geq 0$ such that
   90: 	\begin{equation*}

  182: \section{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:asymptotic}}
  183: 
  184: \subsection{Preliminaries}
  185: 
  186: We need the following conditional version of the Chebotarev density theorem.
  187: 
  188: \begin{theorem}\label{thm:chebotarev}
  189:     Assume the GRH.
  190:     Let $F / \mathbb{Q}$ be a finite Galois extension with Galois group $G$, and let $C$ be a union of conjugacy classes of $G$.
  191:     Then we have that
  192:     \begin{align*}
  193:         \pi_{F, C}(x) &:= \#\big\{p \leq x : p \text{ does not ramify in } F \text{ and } \big[F/\mathbb{Q} \mid p \big] \subseteq C \big\} \\[5pt]
  194:         &\;= \frac{\#C}{\#G} \frac{x}{\log x} + O\!\left(\#C \, x^{1/2} \left(\frac{\log|\Delta_F|}{\#G} + \log x\right)\right) ,
  195:     \end{align*}
  196:     for all $x > 1$.
  197: \end{theorem}
  198: \begin{proof}
  199:     See, e.g.,~\cite[Chapter~2, Section~7]{MR3025442}.
  200: \end{proof}
  201: 
  202: We also need some results on the degree and the discriminant of certain number fields.
  203: 
  204: \begin{lemma}\label{lem:kummer-bounds}
  205:     Let $F$ be a number field, let $a \in F^*$ with $a$ not a root of unity, and let $n$ be a positive integer.
  206:     Then:
  207:     \begin{enumerate}
  208:         \item\label{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1} $[F(\zeta_n, a^{1/n}) : \mathbb{Q}] \gg_{F, a} \varphi(n) n$;
  209:         \item\label{lem:kummer-bounds:ite2} $\log|\Delta_{F(\zeta_n, a^{1/n})}| \ll_{F, a} \varphi(n) n \log(2n)$;
  210:         \item\label{lem:kummer-bounds:ite3} Each prime factor of $\Delta_{F(\zeta_n, a^{1/n})}$ divides $\Norm_F(a) \Delta_F n$.
  211:     \end{enumerate}
  212: \end{lemma}
  213: \begin{proof}
  214:     See, e.g., \cite[Lemma 3 and Lemma 5]{MR2314267}.
  215:     (Claim~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite3} is implicit in the proof of \cite[Lemma 5]{MR2314267}.)
  216: \end{proof}
-----

  182: \section{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:asymptotic}}
  183:

  185: 
  186: We need the following conditional version of the Chebotarev density theorem.
  187: 
  188: \begin{theorem}\label{thm:chebotarev}
  189:     Assume the GRH.
  190:     Let $F / \mathbb{Q}$ be a finite Galois extension with Galois group $G$, and let $C$ be a union of conjugacy classes of $G$.
  191:     Then we have that
  192:     \begin{align*}
  193:         \pi_{F, C}(x) &:= \#\big\{p \leq x : p \text{ does not ramify in } F \text{ and } \big[F/\mathbb{Q} \mid p \big] \subseteq C \big\} \\[5pt]
  194:         &\;= \frac{\#C}{\#G} \frac{x}{\log x} + O\!\left(\#C \, x^{1/2} \left(\frac{\log|\Delta_F|}{\#G} + \log x\right)\right) ,
  195:     \end{align*}
  196:     for all $x > 1$.
  197: \end{theorem}
  198: \begin{proof}
  199:     See, e.g.,~\cite[Chapter~2, Section~7]{MR3025442}.
  200: \end{proof}
  201: 
  202: We also need some results on the degree and the discriminant of certain number fields.
  203: 
  204: \begin{lemma}\label{lem:kummer-bounds}
  205:     Let $F$ be a number field, let $a \in F^*$ with $a$ not a root of unity, and let $n$ be a positive integer.
  206:     Then:
  207:     \begin{enumerate}
  208:         \item\label{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1} $[F(\zeta_n, a^{1/n}) : \mathbb{Q}] \gg_{F, a} \varphi(n) n$;
  209:         \item\label{lem:kummer-bounds:ite2} $\log|\Delta_{F(\zeta_n, a^{1/n})}| \ll_{F, a} \varphi(n) n \log(2n)$;
  210:         \item\label{lem:kummer-bounds:ite3} Each prime factor of $\Delta_{F(\zeta_n, a^{1/n})}$ divides $\Norm_F(a) \Delta_F n$.
  211:     \end{enumerate}
  212: \end{lemma}
  213: \begin{proof}
  214:     See, e.g., \cite[Lemma 3 and Lemma 5]{MR2314267}.
  215:     (Claim~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite3} is implicit in the proof of \cite[Lemma 5]{MR2314267}.)
  216: \end{proof}

  296: 	\begin{equation*}
  297: 		\gamma^{(p - t)/n} \equiv (\gamma^{1/n})^p \cdot \gamma^{-t/n} \equiv \sigma(\gamma^{1/n}) \cdot \gamma^{-t/n} \equiv \gamma^{t/n} \cdot \gamma^{-t/n} \equiv 1 \pmod {\mathfrak{P}} ,
  298: 	\end{equation*}
  299: 	which, by Lemma~\ref{lem:multiplicative-order}, implies that $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p) \mid (p - t) / n$.
  300: 	Hence, we have that $t = \big(\Delta_K \mid p\big)$ and $n \mid \iota_{\bm{u}}(p)$, as desired.
  301: \end{proof}

  303: The rest of the proof follows closely Hooley's proof of Artin's conjecture under the GRH~\cite{MR207630}, with some minor adaptations.
  304: For each positive integer $t$, let us define
  305: \begin{equation}\label{equ:def-deltaut}
  306:     \delta_{\bm{u}}(t) := \sum_{n \,=\, 1}^\infty \frac{\mu(n)\,\#C_{nt}}{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} .
  307: \end{equation}
  308: Note that the series in~\eqref{equ:def-deltaut} converges absolutely, thanks to Lemma~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds}\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1} and Lemma~\ref{lem:1overphinn-series}.
  309: 
  310: For the rest of this section, we shall tacitly ignore the finitely many prime numbers that divide $a_2 \Delta_K$.
  311: For all $x, y, z > 0$, define the sets
  312: \begin{equation*}
  313:     \mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y) := \big\{p \leq x : t \mid \iota_{\bm{u}}(p) \text{ and } qt \nmid \iota_{\bm{u}}(p) \text{ for every } q \leq y \big\}
  314: \end{equation*}
  315: and
  316: \begin{equation*}
  317:     \mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y, z) := \big\{p \leq x : qt \mid \iota_{\bm{u}}(p) \text{ for some } q \in [y, z] \big\} .
  318: \end{equation*}
  319: Moreover, for every $x > 0$, put $y_1 := (\log x) / 6$, $y_2 := x^{1/2} / (\log x)^2$, and $y_3 := x^{1/2} \log x$.
  320: Then, it follows easily that
  321: \begin{equation}\label{equ:4terms}
  322:     \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x) = \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1) + O\big(\#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1, y_2) + \#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_2, y_3) + \#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_3, x) \big) ,
  323: \end{equation}
  324: for all sufficiently large $x$.
  325: The rest of the proof consists of four lemmas estimating the terms of~\eqref{equ:4terms}.
  326: 
  327: \begin{lemma}\label{lem:main-term}
  328:     Assume the GRH.
  329:     Then
  330:     \begin{equation*}
  331:         \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1) = \delta_{\bm{u}}(t) \, \frac{x}{\log x} + O_{\bm{u}}\!\left(\frac{x}{(\log x)^2}\right) ,
-----
  320: Then, it follows easily that
  321: \begin{equation}\label{equ:4terms}
  322:     \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x) = \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1) + O\big(\#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1, y_2) + \#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_2, y_3) + \#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_3, x) \big) ,
  323: \end{equation}
  324: for all sufficiently large $x$.
  325: The rest of the proof consists of four lemmas estimating the terms of~\eqref{equ:4terms}.
  326: 
  327: \begin{lemma}\label{lem:main-term}
  328:     Assume the GRH.
  329:     Then
  330:     \begin{equation*}
  331:         \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1) = \delta_{\bm{u}}(t) \, \frac{x}{\log x} + O_{\bm{u}}\!\left(\frac{x}{(\log x)^2}\right) ,
  332:     \end{equation*}
  333:     for all positive integers $t$ and for all $x > t^3$.
  334: \end{lemma}
  335: \begin{proof}
  336:     Let $\mathcal{S}(y_1)$ be the set of all positive squarefree integers whose prime factors are not exceeding $y_1$.
  337:     By the inclusion-exclusion principle and by Lemma~\ref{lem:n-divides-iotaup}, we get that
  338:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term1}
  339:         \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1) = \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \mu(n) \, \#\big\{p \leq x : nt \mid \iota_u(p)\big\} = \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \mu(n)\,\pi_{K_{nt}, C_{nt}}(x) .
  340:     \end{equation}
  341:     Moreover, by Theorem~\ref{thm:chebotarev} and Lemma~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds}\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1} and \ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite2}, we have that
  342:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term2}
  343:         \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \mu(n)\,\pi_{K_{nt}, C_{nt}}(x)
  344:         = \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \frac{\mu(n)\,\#C_{nt}}{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} \frac{x}{\log x} + O\!\left(x^{1/2} \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \log(2nt x) \right) .
  345:     \end{equation}
  346:     If $n \in \mathcal{S}(y_1)$ then $n \leq \prod_{p \,\leq\, y_1} p \leq 4^{y_1} \leq x^{1/3}$ (see~\cite[Lemma~2.8]{MR2919246}).
  347:     Consequently, a fortiori, $\#\mathcal{S}(y_1) \leq x^{1/3}$.
  348:     Therefore, also recalling that $t < x^{1/3}$, we get that
  349:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term3}
  350:         \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \log(2nt x) \ll x^{1/3} \log x .
  351:     \end{equation}
  352:     Furthermore, by~\eqref{equ:def-deltaut}, Lemma~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds}\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1}, and Lemma~\ref{lem:1overphinn-series}, we have that
  353:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term4}
  354:         \delta_{\bm{u}}(t) - \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \frac{\mu(n)\,\#C_{nt}}{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} \ll \sum_{n \,>\, y_1} \frac1{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} \ll \sum_{n \,>\, y_1} \frac1{\varphi(n)n} \ll \frac1{y_1} \ll \frac1{\log x}.
  355:     \end{equation}
  356:     Putting together \eqref{equ:main-term1}, \eqref{equ:main-term2}, \eqref{equ:main-term3}, and \eqref{equ:main-term4}, the claim follows.
-----
  325: The rest of the proof consists of four lemmas estimating the terms of~\eqref{equ:4terms}.
  326: 
  327: \begin{lemma}\label{lem:main-term}
  328:     Assume the GRH.
  329:     Then
  330:     \begin{equation*}
  331:         \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1) = \delta_{\bm{u}}(t) \, \frac{x}{\log x} + O_{\bm{u}}\!\left(\frac{x}{(\log x)^2}\right) ,
  332:     \end{equation*}
  333:     for all positive integers $t$ and for all $x > t^3$.
  334: \end{lemma}
  335: \begin{proof}
  336:     Let $\mathcal{S}(y_1)$ be the set of all positive squarefree integers whose prime factors are not exceeding $y_1$.
  337:     By the inclusion-exclusion principle and by Lemma~\ref{lem:n-divides-iotaup}, we get that
  338:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term1}
  339:         \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1) = \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \mu(n) \, \#\big\{p \leq x : nt \mid \iota_u(p)\big\} = \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \mu(n)\,\pi_{K_{nt}, C_{nt}}(x) .
  340:     \end{equation}
  341:     Moreover, by Theorem~\ref{thm:chebotarev} and Lemma~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds}\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1} and \ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite2}, we have that
  342:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term2}
  343:         \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \mu(n)\,\pi_{K_{nt}, C_{nt}}(x)
  344:         = \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \frac{\mu(n)\,\#C_{nt}}{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} \frac{x}{\log x} + O\!\left(x^{1/2} \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \log(2nt x) \right) .
  345:     \end{equation}
  346:     If $n \in \mathcal{S}(y_1)$ then $n \leq \prod_{p \,\leq\, y_1} p \leq 4^{y_1} \leq x^{1/3}$ (see~\cite[Lemma~2.8]{MR2919246}).
  347:     Consequently, a fortiori, $\#\mathcal{S}(y_1) \leq x^{1/3}$.
  348:     Therefore, also recalling that $t < x^{1/3}$, we get that
  349:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term3}
  350:         \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \log(2nt x) \ll x^{1/3} \log x .
  351:     \end{equation}
  352:     Furthermore, by~\eqref{equ:def-deltaut}, Lemma~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds}\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1}, and Lemma~\ref{lem:1overphinn-series}, we have that
  353:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term4}
  354:         \delta_{\bm{u}}(t) - \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \frac{\mu(n)\,\#C_{nt}}{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} \ll \sum_{n \,>\, y_1} \frac1{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} \ll \sum_{n \,>\, y_1} \frac1{\varphi(n)n} \ll \frac1{y_1} \ll \frac1{\log x}.
  355:     \end{equation}
  356:     Putting together \eqref{equ:main-term1}, \eqref{equ:main-term2}, \eqref{equ:main-term3}, and \eqref{equ:main-term4}, the claim follows.
  357: \end{proof}
  358: 
  359: \begin{lemma}\label{lem:Quxy1y2}
  360:     Assume the GRH.
  361:     Then

  352:     Furthermore, by~\eqref{equ:def-deltaut}, Lemma~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds}\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1}, and Lemma~\ref{lem:1overphinn-series}, we have that
  353:     \begin{equation}\label{equ:main-term4}
  354:         \delta_{\bm{u}}(t) - \sum_{n \,\in\, \mathcal{S}(y_1)} \frac{\mu(n)\,\#C_{nt}}{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} \ll \sum_{n \,>\, y_1} \frac1{[K_{nt} : \mathbb{Q}]} \ll \sum_{n \,>\, y_1} \frac1{\varphi(n)n} \ll \frac1{y_1} \ll \frac1{\log x}.

  359: \begin{lemma}\label{lem:Quxy1y2}
  360:     Assume the GRH.
  361:     Then
  362:     \begin{equation*}
  363:         \#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1, y_2) \ll \frac{x}{(\log x)^2} ,
  364:     \end{equation*}
  365:     for all positive integers $t$ and for all $x > t^3$.

  367: \begin{proof}
  368:     If $p \in \mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1, y_2)$ then $qt \mid \iota_{\bm{u}}(p)$ for some $q \in [y_1, y_2]$.
  369:     Consequently, by Lemma~\ref{lem:n-divides-iotaup}, we have that
  370:     \begin{equation*}
  371:         \#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_1, y_2) \leq \sum_{q \,\in\, [y_1, y_2]} \pi_{K_{qt}, C_{qt}}(x) .
  372:     \end{equation*}
  373:     Furthermore, by Theorem~\ref{thm:chebotarev} and Lemma~\ref{lem:kummer-bounds}\ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite1} and \ref{lem:kummer-bounds:ite2}, we get that
  374:     \begin{align*}
  375:         \sum_{q \,\in\, [y_1, y_2]} \pi_{K_{qt}, C_{qt}}(x)
  376:         &= \sum_{q \,\in\, [y_1, y_2]} \frac{\# C_{qt}}{[K_{qt} : \mathbb{Q}]}\frac{x}{\log x} + O\!\left(x^{1/2} \sum_{q \,\in\, [y_1, y_2]} \left(\frac{\log|\Delta_{K_{qt}}|}{[K_{qt} : \mathbb{Q}]} + \log x\right) \right) \\
  377:         &\ll \sum_{q \,\in\, [y_1, y_2]} \frac1{\varphi(qt)qt}\frac{x}{\log x} + O\!\left(x^{1/2} \sum_{q \,\in\, [y_1, y_2]} \log(2qt x) \right) \\
  378:         &\ll \sum_{q \,\geq\, y_1} \frac1{q^2}\frac{x}{\log x} + O\!\left(x^{1/2} \log x \sum_{q \,\leq \, y_2} 1 \right) \\
  379:         &\ll \frac1{y_1}\frac{x}{\log x} + O\!\left(x^{1/2} \log x \, \frac{y_2}{\log y_2}\right) \\
  380:         &\ll \frac{x}{(\log x)^2} ,
  381:     \end{align*}
  382:     where we used the upper bound $\sum_{q \,\geq\, z} 1 / q^2 \ll 1/z$ and Chebyshev's estimate $\sum_{q \,\leq\, z} 1 \ll z / \log z$, which holds for every $z > 1$.
  383: \end{proof}

  385: \begin{lemma}\label{lem:Quxy2y3}
  386:     We have that
  387:     \begin{equation*}
  388:         \#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_2, y_3) \ll \frac{x \log (2\log x)}{\varphi(t)(\log x)^2} ,
  389:     \end{equation*}

  391: \end{lemma}
  392: \begin{proof}
  393:     If $p \in \mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_2, y_3)$ then $qt \mid \iota_{\bm{u}}(p)$ for some prime number $q \in [y_2, y_3]$.
  394:     In particular, we have that $p \equiv \pm 1 \pmod {qt}$.
  395:     Consequently, assuming that $x$ is sufficiently large so that $qt \leq y_3 x^{1/3} < x$, by the Brun--Titchmarsh inequality (see, e.g.,~\cite[Theorem~12.7]{MR2919246}), we get that

  397:         \#\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{u}}(t, x, y_2, y_3) &\leq \sum_{q \,\in\, [y_2, y_3]} \#\big\{p \leq x : p \equiv \pm 1 \!\!\!\pmod {qt}\big\}

  399:         &\ll \frac{x}{\varphi(t)\log x} \sum_{q \,\in\, [y_2, y_3]} \frac1{q} \ll \frac{x\log(2\log x)}{\varphi(t)(\log x)^2} ,
  400:     \end{align*}
  401:     where the last estimate follows from the Mertens theorem.

  51: \begin{abstract}
  52:     Let $\bm{u} = (u_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a Lucas sequence, that is, a sequence of integers satisfying $u_0 = 0$, $u_1 = 1$, and $u_n = a_1 u_{n - 1} + a_2 u_{n - 2}$ for every integer $n \geq 2$, where $a_1$ and $a_2$ are fixed nonzero integers.
  53:     For each prime number $p$ with $p \nmid 2a_2D_{\bm{u}}$, where $D_{\bm{u}} := a_1^2 + 4a_2$, let $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ be the \emph{rank of appearance} of $p$ in $\bm{u}$, that is, the smallest positive integer $k$ such that $p \mid u_k$.
  54:     It is well known that $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ exists and that $p \equiv \big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p \big) \pmod {\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)}$, where $\big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p \big)$ is the Legendre symbol.
  55:     Define the \emph{index of appearance} of $p$ in $\bm{u}$ as $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p) := \left(p - \big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p \big)\right) / \rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$.
  56:     For each positive integer $t$ and for every $x > 0$, let $\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x)$ be the set of prime numbers $p$ such that $p \leq x$, $p \nmid 2a_2 D_{\bm{u}}$, and $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p) = t$.
  57: 
  58:     Under the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis, and under some mild assumptions on $\bm{u}$, we prove that
  59: 	\begin{equation*}
  60:         \#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x) = A\, F_{\bm{u}}(t) \, G_{\bm{u}}(t) \, \frac{x}{\log x} + O_{\bm{u}}\!\left(\frac{x}{(\log x)^2} + \frac{x \log (2\log x)}{\varphi(t) (\log x)^2}\right) ,
  61:     \end{equation*}
  62:     for all positive integers $t$ and for all $x > t^3$, where $A$ is the Artin constant, $F_{\bm{u}}(\cdot)$ is a multiplicative function, and $G_{\bm{u}}(\cdot)$ is a periodic function (both these functions are effectively computable in terms of $\bm{u}$).
  63:     Furthermore, we provide some explicit examples and numerical data.
  64: \end{abstract}
  65: 
  66: \maketitle
  67: 
  68: \section{Introduction}
  69: 
  70: Let $\bm{u} = (u_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a Lucas sequence, that is, a sequence of integers satisfying $u_0 = 0$, $u_1 = 1$, and $u_n = a_1 u_{n - 1} + a_2 u_{n - 2}$ for every integer $n \geq 2$, where $a_1$ and $a_2$ are fixed nonzero integers.
  71: For each prime number $p$ with $p \nmid a_2$, let $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ be the \emph{rank of appearance} of $p$ in $\bm{u}$, that is, the smallest positive integer $k$ such that $p \mid u_k$.
  72: It is well known that $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ exists (for this and other elementary facts on $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$ mentioned in the introduction, see~\cite[Chapter~1]{MR1761897}).
  73: Furthermore, we have that $p \equiv \big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p\big) \pmod {\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)}$ for every prime number $p$ with $p \nmid 2 a_2 D_{\bm{u}}$, where $D_{\bm{u}} := a_1^2 + 4a_2$ and $\big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p\big)$ is the Legendre symbol.
  74: We define the \emph{index of appearance} of $p$ in $\bm{u}$ as $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p) := \left(p - \big(D_{\bm{u}} \mid p \big)\right) / \rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$, which, by the previous consideration, is a positive integer.
  75: Moreover, for each positive integer $t$ and for every $x > 0$, we define
  76: \begin{equation*}
  77: 	\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x) := \big\{p \leq x : p \nmid 2 a_2 D_{\bm{u}},\, \iota_{\bm{u}}(p) = t \big\} .
  78: \end{equation*}
  79: Note that $\rho_{\bm{u}}(p)$, respectively $\iota_{\bm{u}}(p)$, is somehow analog to the multiplicative order $r_g(p)$ modulo $p$, respectively the residual index $i_g(p) := (p - 1) / r_g(p)$ modulo $p$, of a rational number $g$ (assuming that $p$ does not divide the numerator and the denominator of $g$).
  80: In particular, the set $\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x)$ is analog to the set of prime numbers $p$ for which $g$ is a \emph{near-primitive root} modulo $p$, that is, $i_p(g) = t$ for a fixed $t$.
  81: This latter set has been studied by several authors \cite{MR3286520,MR1767657,MR2035537,MR3086966} (see also \cite{MR4231532,MR2314267} for generalizations to number fields).
  82: 
  83: As a first result, we provide a (conditional) asymptotic formula for $\#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x)$.
  84: We remark that the proof is a close adaptation of Hooley's proof of the Artin's conjecture under the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis (GRH)~\cite{MR207630}.
  85: 
  86: \begin{theorem}\label{thm:asymptotic}
  87: 	Assume the GRH.
  88: 	Let $\bm{u} = (u_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a nondegenerate Lucas sequence with nonsquare $D_{\bm{u}}$.
  89: 	Then, for every positive integer $t$, there exists $\delta_{\bm{u}}(t) \geq 0$ such that
  90: 	\begin{equation*}
  91: 		\#\mathcal{P}_{\bm{u}}(t, x) = \delta_{\bm{u}}(t) \, \frac{x}{\log x} + O_{\bm{u}}\!\left(\frac{x}{(\log x)^2} + \frac{x \log (2\log x)}{\varphi(t) (\log x)^2}\right) ,
  92: 	\end{equation*}
  93: 	for all $x > t^3$.
  94: \end{theorem}
  95: 
  96: The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:asymptotic} yields an expression for $\delta_{\bm{u}}(t)$ in terms of an infinite series, which however is not very enlightening.
  97: Let
  98: \begin{equation*}
  99:     A := \prod_p \left(1 - \frac1{p(p - 1)}\right) = 0.3739558136\dots
 100: \end{equation*}
 101: be the \emph{Artin constant}, and let
 102: \begin{equation*}
 103: 	F_h(t) := \frac{(t, h)}{\varphi(t)t} \prod_{\substack{p \,>\, 2 \\ p \,\mid\, t}} \left(1 - \frac{(pt, h)}{p^2(t, h)}\right) \left(1 - \frac{(p, h)}{p(p-1)}\right)^{-1} ,
 104: \end{equation*}
 105: for all positive integers $h$ and $t$.
 106: Note that $F_h(\cdot)$ is a multiplicative function.
 107: Our second result is a more explicit description of $\delta_{\bm{u}}(t)$.
 108: 
 109: \begin{theorem}\label{thm:constant}
 110:     Let $\bm{u} = (u_n)_{n \geq 0}$ be a nondegenerate Lucas sequence such that $D_{\bm{u}}$ is not a square and the discriminant of $\mathbb{Q}(\!  \sqrt{D_{\bm{u}}})$ is not equal to $-3$ or to $-4$.
 111:     Then there exist a positive integer $h$ and a periodic function with rational values $G_{\bm{u}}(\cdot)$ such that
 112:     $\delta_{\bm{u}}(t) = A \, F_{2h}(t) \, G_{\bm{u}}(t)$
 113:     for every positive integer $t$.
 114: \end{theorem}
 115: 

codex
The two primary sources support the expert’s bibliographic boundary. Sanna’s 2022 theorem is unconditional but counts primes with an odd `d` dividing the rank, uniformly only once `x > exp(B e^{8 omega(d)} d^8)`; it does not isolate equality. Sanna’s 2024 theorem assumes GRH and counts a fixed index of appearance `t`, uniformly for `x > t^3`; at the exact-rank relation `p = td ± 1`, taking `x` of order `td` forces `t < d^{1/2}` even before addressing the more serious lack of localization to a single rank.

from sympy import primerange

def rank(p):
    a,b=0,1
    for n in range(1, 6*p+7):
        a,b=b,(a+b)%p
        if a==0:
            return n
    raise RuntimeError(p)

for d in (3,5,7,9,11):
    div=[]; exact=[]
    for p in primerange(2,10000):
        r=rank(p)
        if r%d==0:
            div.append((p,r))
        if r==d:
            exact.append(p)
    witness=next(((p,r) for p,r in div if r"'!=d), None)
    print(f"d={d}: #{{p<10000:d|alpha(p)}}={len(div)}, #{{p<10000:alpha(p)=d}}={len(exact)}, strict-witness={witness}")

 3640: \section{Further questions}\label{sec:questions}
 3641: %% ====================================================================
 3642: 
 3643: The preceding sections complete the divisor-lattice theorem chain for this
 3644: article.  We record only two boundary markers; neither is used as evidence for
 3645: the results proved above.
 3646: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
 3647: \item Classify connected support kernels of size at least four. By
 3648: Corollary~\ref{cor:four-support-reduction}, the one-, two-, and
 3649: three-coordinate classifications already determine every disconnected
 3650: four-coordinate pattern. Corollary~\ref{cor:theta-refined-connected-entropy}
 3651: shows that connected kernels already realize the theta-refined lower bound of
 3652: the fixed-choice rank-pure minimal-cover sector, so a full connected
 3653: high-support classification is not a lower-order residual problem within that
 3654: sector. It would be a separate project.
 3655: \item Prove or disprove either rank-window sparsity conjecture. For the mean
 3656: hypothesis, Theorem~\ref{thm:mean-rank-window-deaggregation} reduces the
 3657: problem to the visible single-rank maximum
 3658: \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}; Proposition
 3659: \ref{prop:blms-obstructs-mean-window} also shows that a published conjecture
 3660: predicts failure rather than sparsity. As explained in
 3661: Remark~\ref{rem:exact-average-open-interface}, the remaining question is an
 3662: upper distribution problem for exact-rank prime multiplicities.
 3663: The paper derives conditional normal and mean orders, but does not
 3664: claim that either conjecture follows from presently known primitive-divisor
 3665: theorems, RH, or ERH.
 3666: \end{enumerate}

 3669: \appendix
 3670: 
 3671: \section{Finite verification for the three-coordinate table}
 3672: \label{app:three-coordinate-checklist}
 3673: 
 3674: \begin{proposition}[Reader's checklist for the three-coordinate table]
 3675: \label{prop:three-coordinate-table-checklist}
 3676: Assume $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,
 3677: \ell_2,\ell_3\}$.  The following finite procedure returns exactly
 3678: $\mathcal M_n$.
 3679: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 3680: \item For every nonempty $J\subseteq[3]$, compute the exact-rank window
 3681: $\mathcal R_J^\circ(n)$ and the rank classes
 3682: $\Pi_\alpha(d)$ for $d\in\mathcal R_J^\circ(n)$ by
 3683: Lemma~\ref{lem:primitive-prime-mobius-count}. This gives every prime
 3684: slot $P_J(n)$.
 3685: \item For each coordinate $i$, compute the single possible ladder candidate
 3686: $\mathcal L_i^*(n)$ from
 3687: Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-arithmetic-tests}, then retain it in
 3688: $L_{i,J}(n)$ exactly for those $J$ for which its rank divides $n$ and has
 3689: full-coordinate set $J$.
 3690: \item For each coordinate permutation of each of the eight rows in
 3691: Table~\ref{tab:three-coordinate-classification}, replace the displayed slots
 3692: by the finite sets found in (i) and (ii), with
 3693: $S_i(n)=P_{\{i\}}(n)\cup L_{i,\{i\}}(n)$.
 3694: \item Keep precisely those row fillings whose selected prime powers have
 3695: distinct underlying primes, equivalently those satisfying the Hall
 3696: inequalities in Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-slot-selection}; multiply the selected
 3697: prime powers and insert the product into a set, thereby removing repetitions
 3698: caused by stabilizers and coordinate permutations.
 3699: \end{enumerate}
 3700: The products retained in (iv) are exactly the minimal generators listed in
 3701: Theorem~\ref{thm:low-support-classification-package}.  In particular, the
 3702: table is a closed finite classification for fixed $n$, not an interface to an
 3703: additional infinite search.
 3704: \end{proposition}
 3705: 
 3706: \begin{proof}
 3707: Step (i) is precisely the prime-slot identity in
 3708: Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-arithmetic-tests}; the M\"obius formula
 3709: there is Lemma~\ref{lem:primitive-prime-mobius-count}.  Step (ii) is the
 3710: ladder-slot identity in the same proposition, which says that every
 3711: $L_{i,J}(n)$ is empty or consists of the displayed single candidate.  Step
 3712: (iii) is only the coordinate-labelling convention of
 3713: Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-nonemptiness}.  By that theorem, after the
 3714: slots of a labelled row are replaced by their finite admissible sets, a row
 3715: realizes a minimal generator if and only if the slot-selection test in step
 3716: (iv) succeeds; Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-slot-selection} is exactly the stated
 3717: Hall criterion for distinct underlying primes.  The one-factor rows
 3718: $\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_3$ are the atomic generators with full
 3719: three-coordinate support.  Conversely, every minimal generator with
 3720: three-coordinate support has, by
 3721: Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-realization}, one fixed labelled
 3722: coordinate permutation of one of the eight row types and therefore appears in
 3723: this procedure. The labelled atomic factor set is unique, whereas the
 3724: unlabelled row is an $S_3$-orbit; the set insertion in step (iv) prevents the
 3725: raw permuted enumeration from asserting stronger uniqueness.
 3726: \end{proof}
 3727: 
 3728: \begin{example}[Applying the checklist at $n=42$]
 3729: \label{ex:n42-three-coordinate-checklist}
 3730: Let $n=42=2\cdot3\cdot7$ and label the coordinates by
 3731: $(\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3)=(2,3,7)$.  The prime factors of
 3732: \[
 3733: F_{42}=2^3\cdot13\cdot29\cdot211\cdot421
 3734: \]
 3735: have ranks
 3736: \[
 3737: \alpha(2)=3,
 3738: \alpha(4)=6,
 3739: \alpha(13)=7,
 3740: \alpha(29)=14,
 3741: \alpha(421)=21,
 3742: \alpha(211)=42.
 3743: \]
 3744: Thus $\Atom(42)=\{211\}$. The exact-rank windows used below are
 3745: $P_{12}(42)=\varnothing$, $P_{13}(42)=\{29\}$,
 3746: $P_{23}(42)=\{421\}$, and $P_{123}(42)=\{211\}$.  Singleton slots are
 3747: $S_1(42)=\varnothing$, $S_2(42)=\{2\}$, and $S_3(42)=\{13\}$.  The proper
 3748: ladder slots relevant here are
 3749: $L_{1,12}(42)=\{4\}$, $L_{1,123}(42)=\varnothing$,
 3750: $L_{2,J}(42)=\varnothing$, and $L_{3,J}(42)=\varnothing$.
 3751: 
 3752: The table then gives the following non-atomic generators.  The row
 3753: $\Gamma_4$ with the permuted slots $P_{13}$ and $P_{23}$ gives
 3754: $29\cdot421=12209$.  The row $\Gamma_5$ gives
 3755: $4\cdot421=1684$ from $L_{1,12}$ and $P_{23}$, while the analogous
 3756: coordinate permutation with an exact-rank $P_{13}$ partner is not a row of type
 3757: $\Gamma_5$ for this ladder orientation.  The row $\Gamma_7$ gives
 3758: $2\cdot29=58$ from $S_2$ and $P_{13}$ after relabeling.  The row $\Gamma_8$
 3759: gives $13\cdot4=52$ from $S_3$ and the permuted ladder slot $L_{1,12}$.
 3760: All other rows fail because one of the required slots is empty.  Hence
 3761: \[
 3762: \mathcal M_{42}=\{52,58,211,1684,12209\}.
 3763: \]
 3764: The example illustrates the theorem's reading rule: every success or failure
 3765: is decided by a finite slot list and a coprimeness check.
 3766: \end{example}
