 2263: %% ====================================================================
 2264: \section{Support entropy and the unresolved arithmetic multiplicity}
 2265: \label{sec:growth}
 2266: %% ====================================================================
 2267: 
 2268: Write $\Omega(N)=\sum_p\nu_p(N)$ for the number of prime factors of $N$
 2269: counted with multiplicity. Set $R(2)=1$, consistently with
 2270: $\mathcal M_2=\varnothing$. For $n\ge3$ define the maximal atomic rank-window
 2271: multiplicity
 2272: \begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-window-multiplicity}
 2273:  R(n):=\max\!\left\{1,
 2274:  \max_{\varnothing\ne I\subseteq J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}
 2275:        \#\mathcal A_{I,J}(n)\right\}.
 2276: \end{equation}
 2277: By the prime--ladder alternative, the nonsingleton families counted here are the
 2278: exact-rank windows $P_J(n)$, whereas every ladder family contributes at most
 2279: one atom. Thus $R(n)$ records the maximal atomic-family multiplicity retained
 2280: by the counting bounds below. It is an exact parameter for those bounds, but
 2281: it is not asserted to be an information-theoretically complete invariant of
 2282: the arithmetic outside the support combinatorics.
 2283: 
 2284: We first remove the contribution caused solely by collecting several exact
 2285: rank classes in one support window. For $d\mid n$, write
 2286: \[
 2287:  T_n(d):=\{\ell\in\mathcal P(n):
 2288:  \nu_\ell(d)=\nu_\ell(n)\}.
 2289: \]
 2290: 
 2291: \begin{theorem}[Mean rank-window deaggregation]
 2292: \label{thm:mean-rank-window-deaggregation}
 2293: Put
 2294: \[
 2295:  a(d):=\#\Pi_\alpha(d),\qquad
 2296:  \mathcal D^+(n):=\{d\mid n:T_n(d)\ne\varnothing\},
 2297: \]
 2298: and
 2299: \[
 2300:  A^*(n):=\max\left\{1,\max_{d\in\mathcal D^+(n)}a(d)\right\}.
 2301: \]
 2302: Then, as $x\to\infty$,
 2303: \begin{equation}\label{eq:mean-window-deaggregation}
 2304:  \sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)\log R(n)
 2305:  =\sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n)
 2306:  +O(x\log\log x).
 2307: \end{equation}
 2308: Consequently the mean sparse-window hypothesis \textup{(H2)} is equivalent to
 2309: \begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}
 2310:  \sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)
 2311:  \log\max\left\{1,
 2312:  \max_{\substack{d\mid n\\T_n(d)\ne\varnothing}}
 2313:  \#\Pi_\alpha(d)\right\}
 2314:  =o\bigl(x(\log\log x)^2\bigr).
 2315: \end{equation}
 2316: \end{theorem}
 2317: 
 2318: \begin{proof}
 2319: For nonempty $J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$, let
 2320: \[
 2321:  W_J(n):=\{d\mid n:T_n(d)=J\}.
 2322: \]
 2323: If $n=\prod_\ell\ell^{e_\ell}$, then
 2324: \begin{equation}\label{eq:support-window-size}
 2325:  \#W_J(n)=\prod_{\ell\notin J}e_\ell.
 2326: \end{equation}
 2327: The prime atoms in the family with essential and full supports $(J,J)$ form
 2328: the disjoint union
 2329: \[
 2330:  \coprod_{d\in W_J(n)}\Pi_\alpha(d),
 2331: \]
 2332: because a prime has a unique rank of apparition. By
 2333: Lemma~\ref{thm:primitive-ladder-dichotomy}, every remaining effective atomic
 2334: family is a ladder family with singleton essential support. Once that
 2335: essential coordinate $\ell$ is fixed, the underlying prime is $\ell$, and
 2336: the full-exponent equation in Proposition~\ref{prop:prime-power-ladders}
 2337: determines at most one ladder exponent. Therefore, with
 2338: \[
 2339:  P(n):=\max_{\varnothing\ne J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}
 2340:        \sum_{d\in W_J(n)}a(d),
 2341: \]
 2342: one has
 2343: \begin{equation}\label{eq:prime-window-versus-R}
 2344:  P(n)\le R(n)\le P(n)+1.
 2345: \end{equation}
 2346: Every $d\in\mathcal D^+(n)$ belongs to one of the windows above. Hence
 2347: \[
 2348:  A^*(n)\le R(n)
 2349:  \le1+A^*(n)\max_J\#W_J(n)
 2350:  \le1+A^*(n)\prod_{\ell^{e_\ell}\parallel n}e_\ell.
 2351: \]
 2352: Writing
 2353: \[
 2354:  h(n):=\sum_{\ell^{e_\ell}\parallel n}\log e_\ell,
 2355: \]
 2356: and using $A^*(n)\ge1$ gives
 2357: \begin{equation}\label{eq:pointwise-window-deaggregation}
 2358:  0\le\log R(n)-\log A^*(n)\le\log2+h(n).
 2359: \end{equation}
 2360: For each exponent $e\ge2$, $\log e\le\sum_{2\le j\le e}\log j$, so
 2361: \[
 2362:  h(n)\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\mid n\\j\ge2}}\log j.
 2363: \]
 2364: The classical mean estimate \eqref{eq:mean-omega-input}, used uniformly with
 2365: $\log\log(3y)$ in place of $\log\log y$ for small $y$, yields
 2366: \begin{align*}
 2367:  \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)h(n)
 2368:  &\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\le x\\j\ge2}}\log j
 2369:        \sum_{m\le x/p^j}\omega(p^jm)\\
 2370:  &\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\le x\\j\ge2}}\log j
 2371:        \sum_{m\le x/p^j}(1+\omega(m))\\
 2372:  &\ll x\log\log x\sum_p\sum_{j\ge2}\frac{\log j}{p^j}
 2373:  \ll x\log\log x.
 2374: \end{align*}
 2375: The double series converges absolutely. Summing
 2376: \eqref{eq:pointwise-window-deaggregation} and using
 2377: $\sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\ll x\log\log x$ proves
 2378: \eqref{eq:mean-window-deaggregation}; the stated equivalence follows because
 2379: $x\log\log x=o(x(\log\log x)^2)$.
 2380: \end{proof}
 2381: 
 2382: \begin{corollary}[Single-rank sufficient and necessary interfaces]
 2383: \label{cor:single-rank-mean-interfaces}
 2384: A sufficient condition for \textup{(H2)} is
 2385: \begin{equation}\label{eq:single-rank-sufficient-condition}
 2386:  \sum_{d\le x}
 2387:  \frac{(\omega(d)+\log\log x)\log\max\{1,a(d)\}}{d}
 2388:  =o\bigl((\log\log x)^2\bigr).
 2389: \end{equation}
 2390: Conversely, \textup{(H2)} implies the diagonal necessary condition
 2391: \begin{equation}\label{eq:single-rank-necessary-condition}
 2392:  \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log\max\{1,a(n)\}
 2393:  =o\bigl(x(\log\log x)^2\bigr).
 2394: \end{equation}
 2395: \end{corollary}
 2396: 
 2397: \begin{proof}
 2398: Since
 2399: \[
 2400:  \log A^*(n)\le\sum_{d\mid n}\log\max\{1,a(d)\},
 2401: \]
 2402: the substitution $n=dm$ and $\omega(dm)\le\omega(d)+\omega(m)$ gives
 2403: \[
 2404:  \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n)
 2405:  \ll x\sum_{d\le x}
 2406:  \frac{(\omega(d)+\log\log x)\log\max\{1,a(d)\}}{d}.
 2407: \]
 2408: Thus \eqref{eq:single-rank-sufficient-condition} and
 2409: Theorem~\ref{thm:mean-rank-window-deaggregation} imply \textup{(H2)}. For the
 2410: necessary condition, $n\in\mathcal D^+(n)$, and hence
 2411: $R(n)\ge A^*(n)\ge\max\{1,a(n)\}$; summation proves the claim.
 2412: \end{proof}
 2413: 
 2414: \begin{theorem}[Fibotomic rank entropy]
 2415: \label{thm:fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 2416: Let $\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2$, let $\phi_{\rm E}$ denote Euler's totient
 2417: function, and, for $d\geq3$, put
 2418: \[
 2419:  \mathfrak F_d:=\prod_{r\mid d}F_r^{\mu(d/r)}.
 2420: \]
 2421: For $k\geq0$, set $m=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$ and
 2422: \[
 2423:  \mathcal H_d(k):=
 2424:  k\log\frac{2d}{3}+2\log(m!)+(k-2m)\log(m+1).
 2425: \]
 2426: There is an absolute constant $C_F$ such that, for every $d\geq3$,
 2427: \begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 2428:  \mathcal H_d(a(d))\leq \phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+C_F.
 2429: \end{equation}
 2430: More generally, every finitely supported family of nonnegative real numbers
 2431: $(\lambda_d)_{d\geq3}$ satisfies
 2432: \begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 2433:  \sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d\mathcal H_d(a(d))
 2434:  \leq (\log\varphi)\sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d\phi_{\rm E}(d)
 2435:        +C_F\sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d.
 2436: \end{equation}
 2437: Uniformly as $d\to\infty$,
 2438: \begin{equation}\label{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound}
 2439:  a(d)\leq
 2440:  \left(\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1)\right)
 2441:  \frac{\phi_{\rm E}(d)}{\log d}.
 2442: \end{equation}
 2443: Consequently, uniformly as $n\to\infty$,
 2444: \begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}
 2445:  A^*(n)\leq
 2446:  \left(\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1)\right)
 2447:  \frac{\phi_{\rm E}(n)}{\log n}.
 2448: \end{equation}
 2449: \end{theorem}
 2450: 
 2451: \begin{proof}
 2452: Let $\Psi_d(x)$ be the $d$th fibotomic polynomial. The fibotomic
 2453: factorization of Byer--Dvorachek--Eckard--Harrington--Wise--Wong
 2454: \cite{CameronCountsLundMillerPiechnikWong2022Fibotomic} gives
 2455: \[
 2456:  F_d(x)=\prod_{r\mid d}\Psi_r(x),
 2457:  \qquad
 2458:  \mathfrak F_d=\Psi_d(1)\in\mathbb N.
 2459: \]
 2460: This published factorization is used as an input and is not asserted as a
 2461: result of the present paper.
 2462: Binet's formula, in the form
 2463: \[
 2464:  F_r=\frac{\varphi^r}{\sqrt5}
 2465:  \bigl(1-(-\varphi^{-2})^r\bigr),
 2466: \]
 2467: and the identities
 2468: $\sum_{r\mid d}\mu(d/r)=0$ and
 2469: $\sum_{r\mid d}r\mu(d/r)=\phi_{\rm E}(d)$ show that
 2470: \begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-binet-error}
 2471:  \log\mathfrak F_d=\phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+E_d,
 2472:  \qquad
 2473:  |E_d|\leq
 2474:  C_F:=\sum_{j\geq1}
 2475:  \left|\log\bigl(1-(-\varphi^{-2})^j\bigr)\right|.
 2476: \end{equation}
 2477: The series defining $C_F$ converges geometrically.
 2478: 
 2479: If $p\in\Pi_\alpha(d)$, then $p\mid F_d$ but $p\nmid F_r$ for every
 2480: proper divisor $r$ of $d$. Since
 2481: $F_d=\prod_{r\mid d}\mathfrak F_r$ and each factor is an integer, $p$ must
 2482: divide $\mathfrak F_d$. Distinct exact-rank primes are coprime, and hence
 2483: \begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-rank-radical-divides-fibotomic}
 2484:  \prod_{p\in\Pi_\alpha(d)}p\mid\mathfrak F_d.
 2485: \end{equation}
 2486: For $p\notin\{2,5\}$, the classical rank congruence gives
 2487: \[
 2488:  d=\alpha(p)\mid p-\left(\frac5p\right),
 2489: \]
 2490: see \cite{Wall1960FibonacciModm,Vinson1963RankApparition}. Thus
 2491: $p\equiv\pm1\pmod d$. The exceptional ranks $d=3,5$ have respectively
 2492: the single exact-rank primes $2,5$ and satisfy the estimate directly. For
 2493: every other $d$, write the exact-rank primes in increasing order as
 2494: $p_1<\cdots<p_k$. There are at most two candidates in each pair of residue
 2495: classes $jd\pm1$, whence
 2496: \[
 2497:  p_i\geq d\left\lceil\frac i2\right\rceil-1
 2498:  \geq\frac{2d}{3}\left\lceil\frac i2\right\rceil.
 2499: \]
 2500: It follows that, with $m=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$,
 2501: \begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-congruence-product-bound}
 2502:  \prod_{i=1}^kp_i\geq
 2503:  \left(\frac{2d}{3}\right)^k(m!)^2(m+1)^{k-2m}.
 2504: \end{equation}
 2505: Combining \eqref{eq:fibotomic-binet-error}--
 2506: \eqref{eq:rank-congruence-product-bound} and taking logarithms proves
 2507: \eqref{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}. Multiplication by $\lambda_d\geq0$ and
 2508: finite summation proves \eqref{eq:weighted-fibotomic-rank-entropy}.
 2509: 
 2510: Stirling's lower bound applied in \eqref{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy} gives,
 2511: with an absolute implied constant,
 2512: \begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-entropy-coarse}
 2513:  a(d)\bigl(\log d+\log\max\{1,a(d)\}-O(1)\bigr)
 2514:  \leq \phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+O(1).
 2515: \end{equation}
 2516: If $a(d)\leq\phi_{\rm E}(d)/(\log d)^2$, then
 2517: \eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} follows immediately. Otherwise the
 2518: standard uniform lower bound
 2519: $\phi_{\rm E}(d)\gg d/\log\log(3d)$
 2520: \cite{HardyWright2008Introduction} implies
 2521: \[
 2522:  \log a(d)=\log d+O(\log\log d)=\log d+o(\log d).
 2523: \]
 2524: Substitution in \eqref{eq:rank-entropy-coarse} yields
 2525: $(2+o(1))a(d)\log d\leq\phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi$, proving
 2526: \eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} uniformly.
 2527: 
 2528: For \eqref{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}, the case $A^*(n)=1$ follows from
 2529: $\phi_{\rm E}(n)/\log n\to\infty$. Otherwise choose
 2530: $d_n\in\mathcal D^+(n)$ with $A^*(n)=a(d_n)$. The preceding estimate has
 2531: the coarse consequence $a(d)\ll d/\log(2d)$. If
 2532: $d_n\leq n/(\log n)^3$, the lower bound for $\phi_{\rm E}(n)$ gives
 2533: \[
 2534:  a(d_n)=o\left(\frac{\phi_{\rm E}(n)}{\log n}\right).
 2535: \]
 2536: If $d_n>n/(\log n)^3$, then
 2537: $\log d_n=(1+o(1))\log n$ and, since $d_n\mid n$,
 2538: $\phi_{\rm E}(d_n)\leq\phi_{\rm E}(n)$. Applying
 2539: \eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} at $d_n$ completes the proof.
 2540: \end{proof}
 2541: 
 2542: \begin{corollary}[Bounds for the visible single-rank contribution]
 2543: \label{cor:visible-rank-sum-bounds}
 2544: Put
 2545: \[
 2546:  S(x):=\sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n).
 2547: \]
 2548: Then
 2549: \begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-rank-sum-upper}
 2550:  S(x)\leq
 2551:  \sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log\phi_{\rm E}(n)
 2552:  -x(\log\log x)^2+O(x\log\log x),
 2553: \end{equation}
 2554: whereas
 2555: \begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-rank-sum-lower}
 2556:  S(x)\geq
 2557:  \left(\frac{2\log2}{25}+o(1)\right)x\log\log x.
 2558: \end{equation}
 2559: Neither estimate settles \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
 2560: \end{corollary}
 2561: 
 2562: \begin{proof}
 2563: Taking logarithms in \eqref{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}, uniformly for
 2564: large $n$, gives
 2565: \[
 2566:  \log A^*(n)\leq
 2567:  \log\phi_{\rm E}(n)-\log\log n
 2568:  +\log\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1).
 2569: \]
 2570: In addition to \eqref{eq:mean-omega-input}, partial summation gives
 2571: \[
 2572:  \sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log\log n
 2573:  =x(\log\log x)^2+O(x\log\log x).
 2574: \]
 2575: For example, on $\sqrt x<n\leq x$ one may replace $\log\log n$ by
 2576: $\log\log x$ with $O(1)$ error, while the complementary range contributes
 2577: $o(x)$. Summation proves \eqref{eq:visible-rank-sum-upper}.
 2578: 
 2579: For the lower bound, Jarden proved that $L_{5p}$ has at least two distinct
 2580: primitive prime divisors for every prime $p>5$
 2581: \cite{Jarden1968LucasTwoPrimitive}; this published theorem is used as an
 2582: input and is not asserted as a result of the present paper. If $q$ is one of
 2583: them, then
 2584: $q\mid L_{5p}$ and
 2585: \[
 2586:  F_{10p}=F_{5p}L_{5p},\qquad \gcd(F_{5p},L_{5p})\mid2.
 2587: \]
 2588: Here $q$ is odd, so the gcd identity gives $q\nmid F_{5p}$. Thus
 2589: $\alpha(q)=2r$ for some $r\mid5p$. If $r<5p$, then
 2590: $F_{2r}=F_rL_r$ and $q\nmid F_r$, so $q\mid L_r$, contrary to the
 2591: primitivity of $q$ for $L_{5p}$.
 2592: Consequently $\alpha(q)=10p$, and therefore
 2593: \begin{equation}\label{eq:jarden-exact-rank-consequence}
 2594:  a(10p)\geq2\qquad(p>5\ \hbox{prime}).
 2595: \end{equation}
 2596: 
 2597: Let $n=10m$, where $5\nmid m$ and $m$ has a prime factor $p>5$. Then
 2598: $d=10p$ divides $n$ and
 2599: $\nu_5(d)=\nu_5(n)=1$, so $T_n(d)\ne\varnothing$. By
 2600: \eqref{eq:jarden-exact-rank-consequence}, $A^*(n)\geq2$. The excluded
 2601: integers $m$ having no prime factor greater than $5$ are of the form
 2602: $2^a3^b$ and contribute only $O((\log x)^3)$ after weighting by $\omega$.
 2603: Writing $y=x/10$ and interchanging two finite sums gives
 2604: \[
 2605:  \sum_{\substack{m\leq y\\5\nmid m}}\omega(m)
 2606:  =\sum_{\substack{q\leq y\\q\ \mathrm{prime},\ q\ne5}}
 2607:  \left(\left\lfloor\frac yq\right\rfloor
 2608:  -\left\lfloor\frac y{5q}\right\rfloor\right)
 2609:  =\frac45y\log\log y+O(y).
 2610: \]
 2611: Since $\omega(10m)=\omega(m)+O(1)$, the total $\omega$-weight of the
 2612: constructed integers is
 2613: $(2/25)x\log\log x+O(x)$. Multiplication by $\log2$ proves
 2614: \eqref{eq:visible-rank-sum-lower}.
 2615: \end{proof}
 2616: 
 2617: 
 2618: For a finite set $V$, let $\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ denote the set of
 2619: minimal, or irredundant, covers of $V$ by nonempty subsets:
 2620: \[
 2621:  \operatorname{MinCov}(V):=
 2622:  \left\{\mathcal C\subseteq 2^V\setminus\{\varnothing\}:
 2623:  \bigcup_{S\in\mathcal C}S=V,\quad
 2624:  S\nsubseteq\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal C\setminus\{S\}}T
 2625:  \ \text{for every }S\in\mathcal C\right\}.
 2626: \]
 2627: 
 2628: \begin{theorem}[Rank-pure universality]\label{thm:rank-pure-universality}
 2629: Let $n\ge3$, put $V=\mathcal P(n)$, and, for every nonempty $S\subseteq V$,
 2630: define
 2631: \[
 2632:  n_S:=\prod_{\ell\in S}\ell^{\nu_\ell(n)},\qquad
 2633:  a_n(S):=\#\Pi_\alpha(n_S),
 2634: \]
 2635: and
 2636: \[
 2637:  \mathcal E_n:=\{S\subseteq V:S\ne\varnothing,\ n_S\in\{2,6,12\}\}.
 2638: \]
 2639: Then $\#\mathcal E_n\le2$, and every member of $\mathcal E_n$ has cardinality
 2640: at most two. Define the rank-pure prime sector
 2641: \[
 2642:  \mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}:=
 2643:  \left\{
 2644:  \prod_{S\in\mathcal C}p_S:
 2645:  \begin{array}{l}
 2646:   \mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V),\quad
 2647:   \mathcal C\cap\mathcal E_n=\varnothing,\\
 2648:   p_S\in\Pi_\alpha(n_S)\quad(S\in\mathcal C)
 2649:  \end{array}
 2650:  \right\}.
 2651: \]
 2652: Then $\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}\subseteq\mathcal M_n$, the displayed representation is
 2653: unique, and
 2654: \begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-pure-weighted-sector}
 2655:  \#\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}=
 2656:  \sum_{\substack{\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V)\\
 2657:                    \mathcal C\cap\mathcal E_n=\varnothing}}
 2658:  \prod_{S\in\mathcal C}a_n(S),
 2659:  \qquad
 2660:  a_n(S)=\sum_{d\mid n_S}\mu(n_S/d)\,\omega(F_d).
 2661: \end{equation}
 2662: Moreover, the support hypergraph of the product attached to $\mathcal C$ has
 2663: edge set exactly $\mathcal C$. In particular, connected minimal covers map to
 2664: $\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}$, with their complete hypergraph structure preserved.
 2665: \end{theorem}
 2666: 
 2667: \begin{proof}
 2668: The map $S\mapsto n_S$ is injective. If $n_S=2$, then
 2669: $S=\{2\}$ and $\nu_2(n)=1$. If $n_S=6$, then
 2670: $S=\{2,3\}$ and $\nu_2(n)=\nu_3(n)=1$; if $n_S=12$, then
 2671: $S=\{2,3\}$, $\nu_2(n)=2$, and $\nu_3(n)=1$. Thus the ranks $6$ and $12$
 2672: cannot both occur, while rank $2$ can coexist only with rank $6$. This proves
 2673: the assertions about $\mathcal E_n$.
 2674: 
 2675: Fix $\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ avoiding $\mathcal E_n$. For
 2676: every $S\in\mathcal C$, the integer $n_S$ is at least $3$ and is not $6$ or
 2677: $12$. Theorem~\ref{thm:primitive-divisors} therefore gives a prime
 2678: $p_S\in\Pi_\alpha(n_S)$. Distinct supports have distinct ranks, so the primes
 2679: $p_S$ are distinct. Since $p_S$ is a prime atom and $d^-(p_S)=1$,
 2680: \[
 2681:  E_n(p_S)=T_n(p_S)=S.
 2682: \]
 2683: Consequently the coverage and private-coordinate conditions defining
 2684: $\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ are exactly the witness-cover conditions for
 2685: $\{p_S:S\in\mathcal C\}$. Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-classification} places
 2686: $\prod_{S\in\mathcal C}p_S$ in $\mathcal M_n$.
 2687: 
 2688: Unique factorization recovers every selected prime from the product; its rank
 2689: $\alpha(p_S)=n_S$ then recovers $S$. Hence both the cover and all prime choices
 2690: are recovered, proving injectivity and the weighted count in
 2691: \eqref{eq:rank-pure-weighted-sector}. The formula for $a_n(S)$ is
 2692: Lemma~\ref{lem:primitive-prime-mobius-count}. Finally,
 2693: \[
 2694:  \operatorname{supp}_n(\alpha(p_S))=
 2695:  \operatorname{supp}_n(n_S)=S,
 2696: \]
 2697: so Definition~\ref{def:support-hypergraph} gives precisely the edge set
 2698: $\mathcal C$ and therefore preserves connected components.
 2699: \end{proof}
 2700: 
 2701: \begin{corollary}[Odd-layer cover universality]
 2702: \label{cor:odd-rank-pure-universality}
 2703: Let $n\ge3$ be odd and put $k=\omega(n)$. Then $\mathcal E_n=\varnothing$.
 2704: After fixing one prime $\pi_S\in\Pi_\alpha(n_S)$ for every nonempty
 2705: $S\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$, the map
 2706: \[
 2707:  \mathcal C\longmapsto\prod_{S\in\mathcal C}\pi_S
 2708: \]
 2709: embeds $\operatorname{MinCov}(\mathcal P(n))$ into $\mathcal M_n$ and
 2710: preserves the support hypergraph. Hence, if $C_k$ denotes the number of minimal
 2711: covers of a labelled $k$-set, then
 2712: \begin{equation}\label{eq:odd-minimal-cover-bound}
 2713:  \#\mathcal M_n\ge C_k,
 2714:  \qquad
 2715:  \widthop(B_n)\ge C_k.
 2716: \end{equation}
 2717: Through Royle's correspondence
 2718: \cite{Royle2000SetCoversSplitGraphs}, every isomorphism type of split graph on
 2719: $k$ vertices consequently has a rank-pure Fibonacci realization on the prime
 2720: coordinates of $n$.
 2721: \end{corollary}
 2722: 
 2723: \begin{proof}
 2724: Every nonempty $n_S$ is odd and at least $3$, so no support is exceptional.
 2725: Theorem~\ref{thm:rank-pure-universality} gives the embedding and support
 2726: preservation. Minimal generators form an antichain, which proves the width
 2727: bound. Royle's theorem is a correspondence between isomorphism types of split
 2728: graphs and minimal covers; choosing a labelling of a representative cover and
 2729: then applying the embedding gives the final assertion.
 2730: \end{proof}
 2731: 
 2732: We next fix the classical enumeration input, without claiming it as a result of
 2733: this paper. Let $C_{k,s}$ count the minimal covers of a labelled $k$-set having
 2734: $s$ members, put $C_k=\sum_s C_{k,s}$, and put
 2735: \[
 2736:  b_k:=\sum_{s=0}^k\binom{k}{s}2^{s(k-s)}.
 2737: \]
 2738: Hearne and Wagner \cite{HearneWagner1973MinimalCovers} proved
 2739: \begin{equation}\label{eq:hearne-wagner}
 2740:  C_{k,s}=\sum_{j=s}^k\binom{k}{j}
 2741:  \genfrac{\{}{\}}{0pt}{}{j}{s}
 2742:  (2^s-s-1)^{k-j}.
 2743: \end{equation}
 2744: The central-sum asymptotic occurring in the labelled split-graph enumeration of
 2745: Bender--Richmond--Wormald \cite{BenderRichmondWormald1985ChordalSplit}, in the
 2746: form recorded also by Troyka \cite[Section~3]{Troyka2019SplitGraphs}, gives,
 2747: with
 2748: \[
 2749:  \vartheta_0:=\sum_{r\in\mathbb Z}2^{-r^2},\qquad
 2750:  \vartheta_1:=\sum_{r\in\mathbb Z}2^{-(r+1/2)^2},\qquad
 2751:  \varepsilon\equiv k\pmod2,
 2752: \]
 2753: the published asymptotic
 2754: \begin{equation}\label{eq:minimal-cover-theta}
 2755:  C_k\sim b_k\sim
 2756:  \vartheta_\varepsilon\binom{k}{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}2^{k^2/4}.
 2757: \end{equation}
 2758: In particular, $b_{k-1}/b_k=O(2^{-k/2})$.
 2759: 
 2760: \begin{lemma}[Connected minimal-cover concentration]
 2761: \label{lem:connected-minimal-cover-concentration}
 2762: If $C_k^{\rm conn}$ denotes the number of connected minimal covers, then
 2763: \begin{equation}\label{eq:connected-minimal-cover-asymptotic}
 2764:  C_k-C_k^{\rm conn}=O(kC_{k-1})=o(C_k).
 2765: \end{equation}
 2766: \end{lemma}
 2767: 
 2768: \begin{proof}
 2769: Connected components of a minimal cover are themselves minimal covers, so the
 2770: labelled exponential formula gives
 2771: \[
 2772:  C_k=\sum_{j=1}^k\binom{k-1}{j-1}C_j^{\rm conn}C_{k-j}.
 2773: \]
 2774: The terms $j=1$ and $j=k-1$ contribute $O(kC_{k-1})$. Choosing the least
 2775: private element in each cover member gives $C_j\le b_j\le2^j2^{j^2/4}$.
 2776: For the remaining terms, use this bound and the central lower bound for
 2777: $C_{k-1}$ furnished by the $s=\lfloor(k-1)/2\rfloor$ term of
 2778: \eqref{eq:hearne-wagner}. After division by $kC_{k-1}$, the term indexed by
 2779: $j$ is
 2780: \[
 2781:  \ll \binom{k}{j}
 2782:  2^{-(j-1)(k-j-1)/2}\qquad(2\le j\le k-2).
 2783: \]
 2784: The sum of these bounds is bounded (and in fact tends to zero), by symmetry
 2785: and separation into $2\le j\le k/2$ and its reflection. This proves the first
 2786: estimate in \eqref{eq:connected-minimal-cover-asymptotic}; the second equality
 2787: follows from \eqref{eq:minimal-cover-theta}.
 2788: \end{proof}
 2789: 
 2790: \begin{corollary}[Theta-refined connected support entropy]
 2791: \label{cor:theta-refined-connected-entropy}
 2792: Uniformly along every sequence of integers for which $k=\omega(n)\to\infty$,
 2793: \begin{equation}\label{eq:theta-connected-lower-bound}
 2794:  \#\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\ge
 2795:  (1-o(1))\,
 2796:  \vartheta_\varepsilon\binom{k}{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}2^{k^2/4},
 2797:  \qquad \varepsilon\equiv k\pmod2.
 2798: \end{equation}
 2799: Consequently
 2800: \begin{equation}\label{eq:theta-connected-log-lower-bound}
 2801:  \log\#\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\ge
 2802:  \frac{\log2}{4}k^2+k\log2-\frac12\log k
 2803:  +\log\vartheta_\varepsilon+\frac12\log\frac2\pi+o(1).
 2804: \end{equation}
 2805: The same lower bounds hold with $\mathcal M_n$ in place of
 2806: $\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}$.
 2807: \end{corollary}
 2808: 
 2809: \begin{proof}
 2810: Fix an exceptional support $S_0\in\mathcal E_n$. If a minimal cover contains
 2811: $S_0$, choose a canonical private vertex $x\in S_0$ of that member. Every
 2812: other member avoids $x$ and is recovered from its canonical private vertex and
 2813: an arbitrary subset of the remaining nonprivate vertices. The number of such
 2814: encodings is at most $b_{k-1}$ for each choice of $x$. Therefore the number of
 2815: minimal covers meeting $\mathcal E_n$ is at most
 2816: \[
 2817:  \sum_{S_0\in\mathcal E_n}|S_0|b_{k-1}\le4b_{k-1}=o(C_k).
 2818: \]
 2819: Lemma~\ref{lem:connected-minimal-cover-concentration} also shows that only
 2820: $o(C_k)$ minimal covers are disconnected. Thus $(1-o(1))C_k$ covers are simultaneously
 2821: connected and disjoint from $\mathcal E_n$. Theorem
 2822: \ref{thm:rank-pure-universality} embeds them in
 2823: $\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}$, proving \eqref{eq:theta-connected-lower-bound}.
 2824: Taking logarithms and using
 2825: \[
 2826:  \log\binom{k}{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
 2827:  =k\log2-\frac12\log k+\frac12\log\frac2\pi+o(1)
 2828: \]
 2829: gives \eqref{eq:theta-connected-log-lower-bound}.
 2830: \end{proof}
 2831: 
 2832: \begin{corollary}[Discrete local limit in a fixed-prime rank-pure sector]
 2833: \label{cor:rank-pure-local-limit}
 2834: Let $(n_j)$ be any sequence with $k_j=\omega(n_j)\to\infty$. For every
 2835: $j$ and every nonexceptional nonempty support
 2836: $S\subseteq\mathcal P(n_j)$, fix one prime
 2837: $\pi_{j,S}\in\Pi_\alpha((n_j)_S)$. Let $\mathcal Q_{n_j}^{\rm rp}$ consist of the
 2838: products $\prod_{S\in\mathcal C}\pi_{j,S}$ over all
 2839: $\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(\mathcal P(n_j))$ disjoint from
 2840: $\mathcal E_{n_j}$, and choose $m$ uniformly from $\mathcal Q_{n_j}^{\rm rp}$. Put
 2841: $W_j=\omega(m)$. Along the subsequence $k_j=2q_j$, for every fixed
 2842: $d\in\mathbb Z$,
 2843: \begin{equation}\label{eq:even-rank-pure-local-limit}
 2844:  \Pr(W_j=q_j+d)\longrightarrow\frac{2^{-d^2}}{\vartheta_0}.
 2845: \end{equation}
 2846: Along the subsequence $k_j=2q_j+1$,
 2847: \begin{equation}\label{eq:odd-rank-pure-local-limit}
 2848:  \Pr(W_j=q_j+d)\longrightarrow
 2849:  \frac{2^{-(d-1/2)^2}}{\vartheta_1}.
 2850: \end{equation}
 2851: In particular, $W_j=k_j/2+O_{\mathbb P}(1)$.
 2852: \end{corollary}
 2853: 
 2854: \begin{proof}
 2855: Unique factorization and distinct support ranks show that $W_j$ is the number
 2856: of members of the corresponding cover. By the proof of
 2857: Corollary~\ref{cor:theta-refined-connected-entropy}, deleting covers meeting
 2858: $\mathcal E_{n_j}$ changes the uniform distribution in total variation by
 2859: $o(1)$. For $s=\lfloor k/2\rfloor+d$ with fixed $d$, the $j=s$ term of
 2860: \eqref{eq:hearne-wagner} and the canonical-private-element encoding give
 2861: \[
 2862:  \binom{k}{s}(2^s-s-1)^{k-s}
 2863:  \le C_{k,s}\le \binom{k}{s}2^{s(k-s)},
 2864:  \qquad
 2865:  C_{k,s}\sim\binom{k}{s}2^{s(k-s)}.
 2866: \]
 2867: Dividing this by \eqref{eq:minimal-cover-theta} gives
 2868: \eqref{eq:even-rank-pure-local-limit} and
 2869: \eqref{eq:odd-rank-pure-local-limit}. The quadratic limiting tails are
 2870: summable, which is equivalent to tightness on the integer scale.
 2871: \end{proof}
 2872: 
 2873: \begin{remark}\label{rem:connected-lower-not-dominance}
 2874: Corollary~\ref{cor:theta-refined-connected-entropy} proves that connected
 2875: minimal generators alone realize the theta-refined lower bound supplied by the
 2876: unweighted minimal-cover count in the fixed-choice rank-pure sector, including
 2877: its linear and logarithmic corrections. It does not compare the total connected
 2878: and disconnected parts of $\mathcal M_n$, nor does it assert that this sector
 2879: exhausts the consequences of fibotomic information. Such a comparison would
 2880: require upper control of the arithmetic multiplicities in $R(n)$ and is not
 2881: presently available.
 2882: \end{remark}
 2883: 
 2884: \begin{theorem}[Combinatorial support entropy]
 2885: \label{thm:minimal-generator-growth}
 2886: For every integer $n\ge3$, put $k=\omega(n)$ and
 2887: $L(n)=\log\#\mathcal M_n$. Then $\mathcal M_n$ is nonempty. If $k\ge3$ and
 2888: $r=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$, then
 2889: \begin{equation}\label{eq:private-cover-finite-bounds}
 2890:  (2^r-1)^{k-r}

 concentration consequences follow from fibotomic arithmetic is therefore an
 open interface.
 
+The closest distribution theorems do not close this interface.  In the
+notation of a nondegenerate Lucas sequence $U$, Sanna's Theorem~1.1
+\cite{Sanna2022RankDivisibility} gives, unconditionally, an asymptotic formula
+for
+\[
+ \#\{p\le x:d\mid\rho_U(p)\}
+\]
+when $d$ is odd (with one additional discriminant exception) and
+$x>\exp(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8)$.  The condition counted is
+$d\mid\rho_U(p)$, not the exact-rank condition $\rho_U(p)=d$; for example,
+$3\mid\alpha(17)=9$ although $\alpha(17)\ne3$.  Sanna's Theorem~1.1 on the
+index of appearance \cite{Sanna2024IndexAppearance} instead assumes GRH and,
+for a fixed positive integer $t$, counts primes having
+$\iota_U(p)=t$ uniformly for $x>t^3$.  In the Fibonacci case,
+$\alpha(p)=d$ and $\iota_F(p)=t$ imply
+\[
+ p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
+\]
+Taking $x$ on the scale of this candidate prime restricts that theorem to
+$t^2\ll d$, and its cumulative fixed-index count still does not isolate the
+single rank $d$.  Thus neither result controls the growing exact fibers
+$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ required in \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
+

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。
+
+本轮唯一问题改为本文自身的有限结构理论可以无条件处理的下一边界：完成四坐标连通 witness kernel 的穷尽分类及其精确算术实现判据。设 $n\ge2$ 且 $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4\}$。请在 $S_4$ 作用下分类所有可能来自 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的标号支撑族
+\[
+\Delta=\{(I_1,J_1),\ldots,(I_r,J_r)\},
+\qquad \varnothing\ne I_j\subseteq J_j\subseteq[4],
+\]
+其中必须同时满足 prime--ladder 二择一限制 $I_j=J_j$ 或 $|I_j|=1$、覆盖条件 $\bigcup_jJ_j=[4]$、每个原子的私有坐标条件，以及以 $J_j$ 为边的支撑超图连通。必须证明所得轨道表既完备又无重复，而不能仅给出计算机枚举结果或未经证明的列表。
+
+对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。
+

 concentration consequences follow from fibotomic arithmetic is therefore an
 open interface.
 
+The closest distribution theorems do not close this interface.  In the
+notation of a nondegenerate Lucas sequence $U$, Sanna's Theorem~1.1
+\cite{Sanna2022RankDivisibility} gives, unconditionally, an asymptotic formula
+for
+\[
+ \#\{p\le x:d\mid\rho_U(p)\}
+\]
+when $d$ is odd (with one additional discriminant exception) and
+$x>\exp(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8)$.  The condition counted is
+$d\mid\rho_U(p)$, not the exact-rank condition $\rho_U(p)=d$; for example,
+$3\mid\alpha(17)=9$ although $\alpha(17)\ne3$.  Sanna's Theorem~1.1 on the
+index of appearance \cite{Sanna2024IndexAppearance} instead assumes GRH and,
+for a fixed positive integer $t$, counts primes having
+$\iota_U(p)=t$ uniformly for $x>t^3$.  In the Fibonacci case,
+$\alpha(p)=d$ and $\iota_F(p)=t$ imply
+\[
+ p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
+\]
+Taking $x$ on the scale of this candidate prime restricts that theorem to
+$t^2\ll d$, and its cumulative fixed-index count still does not isolate the
+single rank $d$.  Thus neither result controls the growing exact fibers
+$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ required in \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
+
 The primitive-divisor theorems of Carmichael and Bilu--Hanrot--Voutier give
 existence in the Fibonacci setting. Granville proves an odd-multiplicity
 primitive-factor theorem for a class of second-order Lucas recurrences with

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。
+
+本轮唯一问题改为本文自身的有限结构理论可以无条件处理的下一边界：完成四坐标连通 witness kernel 的穷尽分类及其精确算术实现判据。设 $n\ge2$ 且 $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4\}$。请在 $S_4$ 作用下分类所有可能来自 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的标号支撑族

+\qquad \varnothing\ne I_j\subseteq J_j\subseteq[4],
+\]
+其中必须同时满足 prime--ladder 二择一限制 $I_j=J_j$ 或 $|I_j|=1$、覆盖条件 $\bigcup_jJ_j=[4]$、每个原子的私有坐标条件，以及以 $J_j$ 为边的支撑超图连通。必须证明所得轨道表既完备又无重复，而不能仅给出计算机枚举结果或未经证明的列表。

对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。

 concentration consequences follow from fibotomic arithmetic is therefore an
 open interface.
 
+The closest distribution theorems do not close this interface.  In the
+notation of a nondegenerate Lucas sequence $U$, Sanna's Theorem~1.1
+\cite{Sanna2022RankDivisibility} gives, unconditionally, an asymptotic formula
+for
+\[
+ \#\{p\le x:d\mid\rho_U(p)\}
+\]
+when $d$ is odd (with one additional discriminant exception) and
+$x>\exp(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8)$.  The condition counted is
+$d\mid\rho_U(p)$, not the exact-rank condition $\rho_U(p)=d$; for example,
+$3\mid\alpha(17)=9$ although $\alpha(17)\ne3$.  Sanna's Theorem~1.1 on the
+index of appearance \cite{Sanna2024IndexAppearance} instead assumes GRH and,
+for a fixed positive integer $t$, counts primes having
+$\iota_U(p)=t$ uniformly for $x>t^3$.  In the Fibonacci case,
+$\alpha(p)=d$ and $\iota_F(p)=t$ imply
+\[
+ p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
+\]
+Taking $x$ on the scale of this candidate prime restricts that theorem to
+$t^2\ll d$, and its cumulative fixed-index count still does not isolate the
+single rank $d$.  Thus neither result controls the growing exact fibers
+$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ required in \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
+

+对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。
+

 concentration consequences follow from fibotomic arithmetic is therefore an
 open interface.
 
+The closest distribution theorems do not close this interface.  In the
+notation of a nondegenerate Lucas sequence $U$, Sanna's Theorem~1.1
+\cite{Sanna2022RankDivisibility} gives, unconditionally, an asymptotic formula
+for
+\[
+ \#\{p\le x:d\mid\rho_U(p)\}
+\]
+when $d$ is odd (with one additional discriminant exception) and
+$x>\exp(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8)$.  The condition counted is
+$d\mid\rho_U(p)$, not the exact-rank condition $\rho_U(p)=d$; for example,
+$3\mid\alpha(17)=9$ although $\alpha(17)\ne3$.  Sanna's Theorem~1.1 on the
+index of appearance \cite{Sanna2024IndexAppearance} instead assumes GRH and,
+for a fixed positive integer $t$, counts primes having
+$\iota_U(p)=t$ uniformly for $x>t^3$.  In the Fibonacci case,
+$\alpha(p)=d$ and $\iota_F(p)=t$ imply
+\[
+ p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
+\]
+Taking $x$ on the scale of this candidate prime restricts that theorem to
+$t^2\ll d$, and its cumulative fixed-index count still does not isolate the
+single rank $d$.  Thus neither result controls the growing exact fibers
+$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ required in \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
+

 existence in the Fibonacci setting. Granville proves an odd-multiplicity
 primitive-factor theorem for a class of second-order Lucas recurrences with

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。
+
+本轮唯一问题改为本文自身的有限结构理论可以无条件处理的下一边界：完成四坐标连通 witness kernel 的穷尽分类及其精确算术实现判据。设 $n\ge2$ 且 $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4\}$。请在 $S_4$ 作用下分类所有可能来自 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的标号支撑族
+\[
+\Delta=\{(I_1,J_1),\ldots,(I_r,J_r)\},
+\qquad \varnothing\ne I_j\subseteq J_j\subseteq[4],
+\]
+其中必须同时满足 prime--ladder 二择一限制 $I_j=J_j$ 或 $|I_j|=1$、覆盖条件 $\bigcup_jJ_j=[4]$、每个原子的私有坐标条件，以及以 $J_j$ 为边的支撑超图连通。必须证明所得轨道表既完备又无重复，而不能仅给出计算机枚举结果或未经证明的列表。
+
+对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。

+The closest distribution theorems do not close this interface.  In the
+notation of a nondegenerate Lucas sequence $U$, Sanna's Theorem~1.1
+\cite{Sanna2022RankDivisibility} gives, unconditionally, an asymptotic formula
+for
+\[
+ \#\{p\le x:d\mid\rho_U(p)\}
+\]
+when $d$ is odd (with one additional discriminant exception) and
+$x>\exp(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8)$.  The condition counted is
+$d\mid\rho_U(p)$, not the exact-rank condition $\rho_U(p)=d$; for example,
+$3\mid\alpha(17)=9$ although $\alpha(17)\ne3$.  Sanna's Theorem~1.1 on the
+index of appearance \cite{Sanna2024IndexAppearance} instead assumes GRH and,
+for a fixed positive integer $t$, counts primes having
+$\iota_U(p)=t$ uniformly for $x>t^3$.  In the Fibonacci case,
+$\alpha(p)=d$ and $\iota_F(p)=t$ imply
+\[
+ p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
+\]
+Taking $x$ on the scale of this candidate prime restricts that theorem to
+$t^2\ll d$, and its cumulative fixed-index count still does not isolate the
+single rank $d$.  Thus neither result controls the growing exact fibers
+$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ required in \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
+

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。
+
+本轮唯一问题改为本文自身的有限结构理论可以无条件处理的下一边界：完成四坐标连通 witness kernel 的穷尽分类及其精确算术实现判据。设 $n\ge2$ 且 $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4\}$。请在 $S_4$ 作用下分类所有可能来自 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的标号支撑族
+\[
+\Delta=\{(I_1,J_1),\ldots,(I_r,J_r)\},
+\qquad \varnothing\ne I_j\subseteq J_j\subseteq[4],
+\]
+其中必须同时满足 prime--ladder 二择一限制 $I_j=J_j$ 或 $|I_j|=1$、覆盖条件 $\bigcup_jJ_j=[4]$、每个原子的私有坐标条件，以及以 $J_j$ 为边的支撑超图连通。必须证明所得轨道表既完备又无重复，而不能仅给出计算机枚举结果或未经证明的列表。
+
+对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。
+
+不要重复之前内容.不要重复其他人已经发表公开的推理过程(定理、推论、猜想、命题及证明), 可以使用其他人的结论并引用.不要中间过程结论.请使用学术化语言表达, 禁止口语.

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。
+
+本轮唯一问题改为本文自身的有限结构理论可以无条件处理的下一边界：完成四坐标连通 witness kernel 的穷尽分类及其精确算术实现判据。设 $n\ge2$ 且 $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4\}$。请在 $S_4$ 作用下分类所有可能来自 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的标号支撑族
+\[
+\Delta=\{(I_1,J_1),\ldots,(I_r,J_r)\},
+\qquad \varnothing\ne I_j\subseteq J_j\subseteq[4],
+\]
+其中必须同时满足 prime--ladder 二择一限制 $I_j=J_j$ 或 $|I_j|=1$、覆盖条件 $\bigcup_jJ_j=[4]$、每个原子的私有坐标条件，以及以 $J_j$ 为边的支撑超图连通。必须证明所得轨道表既完备又无重复，而不能仅给出计算机枚举结果或未经证明的列表。
+
+对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。
+
+不要重复之前内容.不要重复其他人已经发表公开的推理过程(定理、推论、猜想、命题及证明), 可以使用其他人的结论并引用.不要中间过程结论.请使用学术化语言表达, 禁止口语.

 concentration consequences follow from fibotomic arithmetic is therefore an
 open interface.
 
+The closest distribution theorems do not close this interface.  In the
+notation of a nondegenerate Lucas sequence $U$ with nonsquare discriminant
+$\Delta$ and squarefree discriminant part $\Delta_0$, Sanna's Theorem~1.1
+\cite{Sanna2022RankDivisibility} gives, unconditionally, an asymptotic formula
+for
+\[
+ \#\{p\le x:d\mid\rho_U(p)\}
+\]
+when $d$ is odd, with $3\nmid d$ if $\Delta_0=-3$, and
+$x>\exp(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8)$.  The condition counted is
+$d\mid\rho_U(p)$, not the exact-rank condition $\rho_U(p)=d$; for example,
+$3\mid\alpha(17)=9$ although $\alpha(17)\ne3$.  Sanna's Theorem~1.1 on the
+index of appearance \cite{Sanna2024IndexAppearance} instead assumes GRH and,
+for a fixed positive integer $t$, counts primes having
+$\iota_U(p)=t$ uniformly for $x>t^3$.  In the Fibonacci case,
+$\alpha(p)=d$ and $\iota_F(p)=t$ imply
+\[
+ p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
+\]
+Taking $x$ on the scale of this candidate prime restricts that theorem to
+$t^2\ll d$, and its cumulative fixed-index count still does not isolate the
+single rank $d$.  Thus neither result controls the growing exact fibers
+$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ required in \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
+
 The primitive-divisor theorems of Carmichael and Bilu--Hanrot--Voutier give
 existence in the Fibonacci setting. Granville proves an odd-multiplicity
 primitive-factor theorem for a class of second-order Lucas recurrences with

@@ -0,0 +1,12 @@
+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。
+
+本轮唯一问题改为本文自身的有限结构理论可以无条件处理的下一边界：完成四坐标连通 witness kernel 的穷尽分类及其精确算术实现判据。设 $n\ge2$ 且 $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4\}$。请在 $S_4$ 作用下分类所有可能来自 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的标号支撑族
+\[
+\Delta=\{(I_1,J_1),\ldots,(I_r,J_r)\},
+\qquad \varnothing\ne I_j\subseteq J_j\subseteq[4],
+\]
+其中必须同时满足 prime--ladder 二择一限制 $I_j=J_j$ 或 $|I_j|=1$、覆盖条件 $\bigcup_jJ_j=[4]$、每个原子的私有坐标条件，以及以 $J_j$ 为边的支撑超图连通。必须证明所得轨道表既完备又无重复，而不能仅给出计算机枚举结果或未经证明的列表。
+
+对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。
+
+不要重复之前内容.不要重复其他人已经发表公开的推理过程(定理、推论、猜想、命题及证明), 可以使用其他人的结论并引用.不要中间过程结论.请使用学术化语言表达, 禁止口语.

 concentration consequences follow from fibotomic arithmetic is therefore an
 open interface.
 
+The closest distribution theorems do not close this interface.  In the
+notation of a nondegenerate Lucas sequence $U$ with nonsquare discriminant
+$\Delta$ and squarefree discriminant part $\Delta_0$, Sanna's Theorem~1.1
+\cite{Sanna2022RankDivisibility} gives, unconditionally, an asymptotic formula
+for
+\[
+ \#\{p\le x:d\mid\rho_U(p)\}
+\]
+when $d$ is odd, with $3\nmid d$ if $\Delta_0=-3$, and
+$x>\exp(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8)$.  The condition counted is
+$d\mid\rho_U(p)$, not the exact-rank condition $\rho_U(p)=d$; for example,
+$3\mid\alpha(17)=9$ although $\alpha(17)\ne3$.  Sanna's Theorem~1.1 on the
+index of appearance \cite{Sanna2024IndexAppearance} instead assumes GRH and,
+for a fixed positive integer $t$, counts primes having
+$\iota_U(p)=t$ uniformly for $x>t^3$.  In the Fibonacci case,
+$\alpha(p)=d$ and $\iota_F(p)=t$ imply
+\[
+ p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
+\]
+Taking $x$ on the scale of this candidate prime restricts that theorem to
+$t^2\ll d$, and its cumulative fixed-index count still does not isolate the
+single rank $d$.  Thus neither result controls the growing exact fibers
+$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ required in \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
+
 The primitive-divisor theorems of Carmichael and Bilu--Hanrot--Voutier give
 existence in the Fibonacci setting. Granville proves an odd-multiplicity
 primitive-factor theorem for a class of second-order Lucas recurrences with

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。
+
+本轮唯一问题改为本文自身的有限结构理论可以无条件处理的下一边界：完成四坐标连通 witness kernel 的穷尽分类及其精确算术实现判据。设 $n\ge2$ 且 $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4\}$。请在 $S_4$ 作用下分类所有可能来自 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的标号支撑族
+\[
+\Delta=\{(I_1,J_1),\ldots,(I_r,J_r)\},
+\qquad \varnothing\ne I_j\subseteq J_j\subseteq[4],
+\]
+其中必须同时满足 prime--ladder 二择一限制 $I_j=J_j$ 或 $|I_j|=1$、覆盖条件 $\bigcup_jJ_j=[4]$、每个原子的私有坐标条件，以及以 $J_j$ 为边的支撑超图连通。必须证明所得轨道表既完备又无重复，而不能仅给出计算机枚举结果或未经证明的列表。
+
+对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。
+
+不要重复之前内容.不要重复其他人已经发表公开的推理过程(定理、推论、猜想、命题及证明), 可以使用其他人的结论并引用.不要中间过程结论.请使用学术化语言表达, 禁止口语.

+The closest distribution theorems do not close this interface.  In the
+notation of a nondegenerate Lucas sequence $U$ with nonsquare discriminant
+$\Delta$ and squarefree discriminant part $\Delta_0$, Sanna's Theorem~1.1
+\cite{Sanna2022RankDivisibility} gives, unconditionally, an asymptotic formula
+for
+\[
+ \#\{p\le x:d\mid\rho_U(p)\}
+\]
+when $d$ is odd, with $3\nmid d$ if $\Delta_0=-3$, and
+$x>\exp(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8)$.  The condition counted is
+$d\mid\rho_U(p)$, not the exact-rank condition $\rho_U(p)=d$; for example,
+$3\mid\alpha(17)=9$ although $\alpha(17)\ne3$.  Sanna's Theorem~1.1 on the
+index of appearance \cite{Sanna2024IndexAppearance} instead assumes GRH and,
+for a fixed positive integer $t$, counts primes having
+$\iota_U(p)=t$ uniformly for $x>t^3$.  In the Fibonacci case,
+$\alpha(p)=d$ and $\iota_F(p)=t$ imply
+\[
+ p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
+\]
+Taking $x$ on the scale of this candidate prime restricts that theorem to
+$t^2\ll d$, and its cumulative fixed-index count still does not isolate the
+single rank $d$.  Thus neither result controls the growing exact fibers
+$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ required in \eqref{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。

 @article{Marques2012OrderAppearance,
   author  = {Diego Marques},
   title   = {Fixed points of the order of appearance in the {F}ibonacci sequence},

codex
**Per-Claim Verdicts**

1. **ALREADY-IN-PAPER**: “No unconditional average-dispersion, critical-concentration, or correct-scale theorem for the controlled-growth exact-rank fibers was established.”

   The expert response contains no candidate theorem, proof, intermediate lemma, or quantitative estimate from which any of the three requested alternatives could be derived. The manuscript already records the unresolved mean interface as
   \[
   \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n)
   =o\!\left(x(\log\log x)^2\right),
   \qquad
   A^*(n)=\max\!\left\{1,\max_{\substack{d\mid n\\T_n(d)\ne\varnothing}}
   \#\Pi_\alpha(d)\right\},
   \]
   and explicitly states that neither its unconditional upper nor lower bound decides this scale. The conditional normal and mean orders remain marked conditional. The arXiv API and Crossref searches found no exact-fiber theorem closing this interface. The recent preprint arXiv:2604.20014, *Explicit Prime Densities for the Rank of Appearance in Lucas Sequences*, extends rank-divisibility densities to all Lucas sequences and all fixed \(d\), but still treats \(d\mid\rho_U(p)\), not \(\rho_U(p)=d\).

2. **ALREADY-PUBLISHED**: “The unconditional Sanna result controls \(d\mid\alpha(p)\), not \(\alpha(p)=d\).”

   The cited paper is Carlo Sanna, *On the divisibility of the rank of appearance of a Lucas sequence*, **International Journal of Number Theory** 18 (2022), no. 10, 2145–2156, DOI `10.1142/S1793042122501093`, arXiv:2008.12506.

   Its Theorem 1.1 assumes that \(U\) is nondegenerate with nonsquare discriminant \(\Delta\), writes \(\Delta_0\) for its squarefree part and \(h\) for the maximal power exponent of \(\alpha/\beta\) in \(\mathbb Q(\sqrt\Delta)\), and requires \(d\) to be odd, with \(3\nmid d\) when \(\Delta_0=-3\). Unconditionally, for
   \[
   x>\exp\!\bigl(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8\bigr),
   \]
   it proves
   \[
   \mathcal R_U(d;x)
   =\delta_U(d)\operatorname{Li}(x)
   +O_U\!\left(
     \frac{(\omega(d)+1)d}{\varphi(d)}
     \frac{x(\log\log x)^{\omega(d)}}{(\log x)^{9/8}}
   \right),
   \]
   where
   \[
   \mathcal R_U(d;x)=\#\{p\le x:p\nmid a_2,\ d\mid\rho_U(p)\},
   \]
   and
   \[
   \delta_U(d)=\frac1d
   \left(\frac1{(d^\infty,h)}+\eta_U(d)\right)
   \prod_{p\mid d}(1-p^{-2})^{-1},
   \]
   with the paper’s stated piecewise value of \(\eta_U(d)\). GRH is not assumed.

   The distinction is strict, not terminological: direct computation for primes below \(10^4\) gives \(3\mid\alpha(17)=9\), \(5\mid\alpha(11)=10\), \(7\mid\alpha(29)=14\), \(9\mid\alpha(19)=18\), and \(11\mid\alpha(43)=44\), while none of these ranks equals the indicated divisor.

3. **ALREADY-PUBLISHED**: “Sanna’s fixed-index asymptotic assumes GRH and does not supply the required growing exact-rank localization.”

   The cited paper is Carlo Sanna, *On the index of appearance of a Lucas sequence*, **The Ramanujan Journal** 63 (2024), no. 4, 1199–1223, DOI `10.1007/s11139-023-00811-4`, arXiv:2212.06127.

   Its Theorem 1.1 assumes GRH and a nondegenerate Lucas sequence with nonsquare discriminant. For every positive integer \(t\),
   \[
   \#\mathcal P_U(t,x)
   =\delta_U(t)\frac{x}{\log x}
   +O_U\!\left(
      \frac{x}{(\log x)^2}
      +\frac{x\log(2\log x)}{\varphi(t)(\log x)^2}
   \right)
   \]
   uniformly for all \(x>t^3\), where
   \[
   \mathcal P_U(t,x)=
   \{p\le x:p\nmid2a_2D_U,\ \iota_U(p)=t\}.
   \]
   Theorem 1.2 identifies \(\delta_U(t)=A F_{2h}(t)G_U(t)\), subject to the additional exclusion of quadratic-field discriminants \(-3\) and \(-4\).

   For Fibonacci primes outside the finite exceptional set,
   \[
   \alpha(p)=d,\qquad\iota_F(p)=t
   \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
   p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
   \]
   Taking \(x\) on the scale \(td\) makes the published range \(x>t^3\) require \(t^2\ll d\), so it does not cover \(t\) at or beyond the square-root scale. More fundamentally, the theorem is a cumulative count over all ranks having fixed index \(t\); it does not localize to the single finite fiber \(\alpha(p)=d\), even inside its permitted range. The expert’s vague phrase “\(t\) growing substantially” is therefore valid only after this quantitative sharpening.

4. **ALREADY-IN-PAPER**: “The claim that fibotomic information is exhausted is retracted.”

   The manuscript already states that it proves only the consequence of the fibotomic size identity, exact-rank radical divisibility, and \(p\equiv\pm1\pmod d\); it explicitly denies both information exhaustion and optimality within any unspecified information class, noting that such a result would require a precise axiom class and a realizable extremal model. See [main.tex](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_upper_fibers_witness_covers_fibonacci_apparition_fq/main.tex:3303). No stronger assertion requiring weakening was found.

+前两轮所追问的可见单秩最大值平均分散问题现予正式搁置，不得在下一轮继续追问。其障碍已经精确定位：Sanna 关于秩整除分布的无条件定理计数的是满足 $d\mid\alpha(p)$ 的素数，而非有限精确纤维 $\alpha(p)=d$；Sanna 关于出现指标 $\iota_F(p)=t$ 的渐近公式依赖 GRH，且仅在 $x>t^3$ 的范围内一致。当 $\alpha(p)=d$ 时必有 $p=td\pm1$，若令 $x$ 与该候选素数同阶，则该范围至多容许 $t^2\ll d$，并且累计的固定指标计数仍不能局部化到单一秩 $d$。因此，继续要求控制增长精确秩纤维的平均分散或临界集中定理，只会重复一个受 GRH、一致性范围及局部化缺失共同阻断的问题。
+
+本轮唯一问题改为本文自身的有限结构理论可以无条件处理的下一边界：完成四坐标连通 witness kernel 的穷尽分类及其精确算术实现判据。设 $n\ge2$ 且 $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4\}$。请在 $S_4$ 作用下分类所有可能来自 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的标号支撑族
+\[
+\Delta=\{(I_1,J_1),\ldots,(I_r,J_r)\},
+\qquad \varnothing\ne I_j\subseteq J_j\subseteq[4],
+\]
+其中必须同时满足 prime--ladder 二择一限制 $I_j=J_j$ 或 $|I_j|=1$、覆盖条件 $\bigcup_jJ_j=[4]$、每个原子的私有坐标条件，以及以 $J_j$ 为边的支撑超图连通。必须证明所得轨道表既完备又无重复，而不能仅给出计算机枚举结果或未经证明的列表。
+
+对每一个组合类型，必须进一步给出对任意固定 $n$ 成立的充要算术实现判据：primitive slot 必须展开为相应精确秩窗中互不相交的 $\Pi_\alpha(d)$，proper ladder slot 必须由本文的唯一候选机制决定，底层素数互异性必须以完整的 Hall 条件处理。由此应得到 $\omega(n)=4$ 时 $\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}$ 的有限闭式分类；再结合本文已经证明的唯一连通块分解与低支撑分类，推出整个 $\mathcal M_n$ 的有限闭式分类。奇数层的 rank-pure minimal-cover 嵌入、平均秩窗 deaggregation 以及精确秩 radical 对 fibotomic 因子的整除关系，只能作为已经证明的输入、完备性校验或算术槽位上界使用，不得重述为新结论，也不得借此恢复任何关于 exact-rank fiber 分布的未证断言。答案至少应包含一个此前未发表、可独立核验的四坐标连通分类定理及完整证明，并明确说明其相对于本文三坐标分类的实质新增内容。

**Per-Claim Verdicts**

1. **ALREADY-IN-PAPER**: “No unconditional average-dispersion, critical-concentration, or correct-scale theorem for the controlled-growth exact-rank fibers was established.”

   The expert response contains no candidate theorem, proof, intermediate lemma, or quantitative estimate from which any of the three requested alternatives could be derived. The manuscript already records the unresolved mean interface as
   \[
   \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n)
   =o\!\left(x(\log\log x)^2\right),
   \qquad
   A^*(n)=\max\!\left\{1,\max_{\substack{d\mid n\\T_n(d)\ne\varnothing}}
   \#\Pi_\alpha(d)\right\},
   \]
   and explicitly states that neither its unconditional upper nor lower bound decides this scale. The conditional normal and mean orders remain marked conditional. The arXiv API and Crossref searches found no exact-fiber theorem closing this interface. The recent preprint arXiv:2604.20014, *Explicit Prime Densities for the Rank of Appearance in Lucas Sequences*, extends rank-divisibility densities to all Lucas sequences and all fixed \(d\), but still treats \(d\mid\rho_U(p)\), not \(\rho_U(p)=d\).

2. **ALREADY-PUBLISHED**: “The unconditional Sanna result controls \(d\mid\alpha(p)\), not \(\alpha(p)=d\).”

   The cited paper is Carlo Sanna, *On the divisibility of the rank of appearance of a Lucas sequence*, **International Journal of Number Theory** 18 (2022), no. 10, 2145–2156, DOI `10.1142/S1793042122501093`, arXiv:2008.12506.

   Its Theorem 1.1 assumes that \(U\) is nondegenerate with nonsquare discriminant \(\Delta\), writes \(\Delta_0\) for its squarefree part and \(h\) for the maximal power exponent of \(\alpha/\beta\) in \(\mathbb Q(\sqrt\Delta)\), and requires \(d\) to be odd, with \(3\nmid d\) when \(\Delta_0=-3\). Unconditionally, for
   \[
   x>\exp\!\bigl(Be^{8\omega(d)}d^8\bigr),
   \]
   it proves
   \[
   \mathcal R_U(d;x)
   =\delta_U(d)\operatorname{Li}(x)
   +O_U\!\left(
     \frac{(\omega(d)+1)d}{\varphi(d)}
     \frac{x(\log\log x)^{\omega(d)}}{(\log x)^{9/8}}
   \right),
   \]
   where
   \[
   \mathcal R_U(d;x)=\#\{p\le x:p\nmid a_2,\ d\mid\rho_U(p)\},
   \]
   and
   \[
   \delta_U(d)=\frac1d
   \left(\frac1{(d^\infty,h)}+\eta_U(d)\right)
   \prod_{p\mid d}(1-p^{-2})^{-1},
   \]
   with the paper’s stated piecewise value of \(\eta_U(d)\). GRH is not assumed.

   The distinction is strict, not terminological: direct computation for primes below \(10^4\) gives \(3\mid\alpha(17)=9\), \(5\mid\alpha(11)=10\), \(7\mid\alpha(29)=14\), \(9\mid\alpha(19)=18\), and \(11\mid\alpha(43)=44\), while none of these ranks equals the indicated divisor.

3. **ALREADY-PUBLISHED**: “Sanna’s fixed-index asymptotic assumes GRH and does not supply the required growing exact-rank localization.”

   The cited paper is Carlo Sanna, *On the index of appearance of a Lucas sequence*, **The Ramanujan Journal** 63 (2024), no. 4, 1199–1223, DOI `10.1007/s11139-023-00811-4`, arXiv:2212.06127.

   Its Theorem 1.1 assumes GRH and a nondegenerate Lucas sequence with nonsquare discriminant. For every positive integer \(t\),
   \[
   \#\mathcal P_U(t,x)
   =\delta_U(t)\frac{x}{\log x}
   +O_U\!\left(
      \frac{x}{(\log x)^2}
      +\frac{x\log(2\log x)}{\varphi(t)(\log x)^2}
   \right)
   \]
   uniformly for all \(x>t^3\), where
   \[
   \mathcal P_U(t,x)=
   \{p\le x:p\nmid2a_2D_U,\ \iota_U(p)=t\}.
   \]
   Theorem 1.2 identifies \(\delta_U(t)=A F_{2h}(t)G_U(t)\), subject to the additional exclusion of quadratic-field discriminants \(-3\) and \(-4\).

   For Fibonacci primes outside the finite exceptional set,
   \[
   \alpha(p)=d,\qquad\iota_F(p)=t
   \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
   p=td+\left(\frac5p\right)=td\pm1.
   \]
   Taking \(x\) on the scale \(td\) makes the published range \(x>t^3\) require \(t^2\ll d\), so it does not cover \(t\) at or beyond the square-root scale. More fundamentally, the theorem is a cumulative count over all ranks having fixed index \(t\); it does not localize to the single finite fiber \(\alpha(p)=d\), even inside its permitted range. The expert’s vague phrase “\(t\) growing substantially” is therefore valid only after this quantitative sharpening.

4. **ALREADY-IN-PAPER**: “The claim that fibotomic information is exhausted is retracted.”

   The manuscript already states that it proves only the consequence of the fibotomic size identity, exact-rank radical divisibility, and \(p\equiv\pm1\pmod d\); it explicitly denies both information exhaustion and optimality within any unspecified information class, noting that such a result would require a precise axiom class and a realizable extremal model. See [main.tex](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_upper_fibers_witness_covers_fibonacci_apparition_fq/main.tex:3303). No stronger assertion requiring weakening was found.

The two verified bibliography records were added to [references_local.bib](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_upper_fibers_witness_covers_fibonacci_apparition_fq/references_local.bib:229).
